hyper-power series and generalized real analytic functions Thanks: D. Tiwari has been supported by grant P 30407 and P33538 of the Austrian Science Fund FWF Thanks: A. Mukhammadiev has been supported by Grant P30407 and P33538 of the Austrian Science Fund FWF Thanks: P. Giordano has been supported by grants P30407, P34113, P33538 of the Austrian Science Fund FWF
Diksha Tiwari and Akbarali Mukhammadiev and Paolo Giordano

Abstract
. This article is a natural continuation of the paper Tiwari, D., Giordano, P., Hyperseries in the non-Archimedean ring of Colombeau generalized numbers in this journal. We study one variable hyper-power series by analyzing the notion of radius of convergence and proving classical results such as algebraic operations, composition and reciprocal of hyper-power series. We then define and study one variable generalized real analytic functions, considering their derivation, integration, a suitable formulation of the identity theorem and the characterization by local uniform upper bounds of derivatives. On the contrary with respect to the classical use of series in the theory of Colombeau real analytic functions, we can recover several classical examples in a non-infinitesimal set of convergence. The notion of generalized real analytic function reveals to be less rigid both with respect to the classical one and to Colombeau theory, e.g. including classical non-analytic smooth functions with flat points and several distributions such as the Dirac delta. On the other hand, each Colombeau real analytic function is also a generalized real analytic function.



2020 Mathematics Subject Classification
46F-XX, 46F30, 26E3

1.
Introduction

In this article, the study of hyperseries in the non-Archimedean ring
of Colombeau generalized numbers (CGN), as carried out in
[
29
]
,
is applied to the corresponding notion of hyper-power series. As we
will see, this yields results which are more closely related to classical
ones, such as, e.g. the equality
\mathchoice{\mathchoice{{\vphantom{\sum}}{}^{\scalebox{0.6}{$\raisebox{-1.59442pt}{$\rho\hskip-5.49028pt$}$}}}{{\vphantom{\sum}}{}^{\scalebox{0.56}{$\raisebox{-1.59442pt}{$\rho\hskip-5.49028pt$}$}}}{{\vphantom{\sum}}{}^{\scalebox{0.47}{$\raisebox{-1.59442pt}{$\rho\hskip-5.49028pt$}$}}}{{\vphantom{\sum}}{}^{\scalebox{0.35}{$\raisebox{-1.59442pt}{$\rho\hskip-5.49028pt$}$}}}\sum_{n\in\mathchoice{{\vphantom{\mathbb{N}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.6}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{N}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.56}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{N}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.47}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{N}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.35}{$\rho$}}}\widetilde{\mathbb{N}}\,}}{\mathchoice{{\vphantom{\sum}}{}^{\scalebox{0.6}{$\rho\hskip-2.04861pt$}}}{{\vphantom{\sum}}{}^{\scalebox{0.56}{$\rho\hskip-2.04861pt$}}}{{\vphantom{\sum}}{}^{\scalebox{0.47}{$\rho\hskip-2.04861pt$}}}{{\vphantom{\sum}}{}^{\scalebox{0.35}{$\rho\hskip-2.04861pt$}}}\sum_{n\in\mathchoice{{\vphantom{\mathbb{N}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.6}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{N}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.56}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{N}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.47}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{N}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.35}{$\rho$}}}\widetilde{\mathbb{N}}\,}}{\mathchoice{{\vphantom{\sum}}{}^{\scalebox{0.6}{$\rho\hskip-2.04861pt$}}}{{\vphantom{\sum}}{}^{\scalebox{0.56}{$\rho\hskip-2.04861pt$}}}{{\vphantom{\sum}}{}^{\scalebox{0.47}{$\rho\hskip-2.04861pt$}}}{{\vphantom{\sum}}{}^{\scalebox{0.35}{$\rho\hskip-2.04861pt$}}}\sum_{n\in\mathchoice{{\vphantom{\mathbb{N}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.6}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{N}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.56}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{N}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.47}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{N}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.35}{$\rho$}}}\widetilde{\mathbb{N}}\,}}{\mathchoice{{\vphantom{\sum}}{}^{\scalebox{0.6}{$\rho\hskip-2.04861pt$}}}{{\vphantom{\sum}}{}^{\scalebox{0.56}{$\rho\hskip-2.04861pt$}}}{{\vphantom{\sum}}{}^{\scalebox{0.47}{$\rho\hskip-2.04861pt$}}}{{\vphantom{\sum}}{}^{\scalebox{0.35}{$\rho\hskip-2.04861pt$}}}\sum_{n\in\mathchoice{{\vphantom{\mathbb{N}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.6}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{N}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.56}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{N}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.47}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{N}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.35}{$\rho$}}}\widetilde{\mathbb{N}}\,}}\frac{x^{n}}{n!}=e^{x}

that holds for all
x\in\mathchoice{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.6}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.56}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.47}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.35}{$\rho$}}}\widetilde{\mathbb{R}}
where the exponential is moderate,
i.e. if
|x|\leq\log\left(\mathchoice{\hbox{\rm d}\rho}{\hbox{\rm d}\rho}{\scalebox{0.75}{$\hbox{\rm d}\rho$}}{\scalebox{0.35}{$\hbox{\rm d}\rho$}}^{-R}\right)
for some
R\in\mathbb{N}
.
On the other hand, we will see that classical smooth but non-analytic
functions, e.g. smooth functions with flat points, and Schwartz distributions
like the Dirac delta, are now included in the related notion of
generalized
real analytic function
(GRAF). This implies that necessarily we cannot
have a trivial generalization of the identity theorem (see e.g.
[
22
, Cor. 1.2.6, 1.2.7]
)
but, on the contrary, only a suitable sufficient condition (see Thm.
40

below). The notion of generalized real analytic function hence reveals
to be less rigid than the classical concept, by including a large
family of non-trivial generalized functions (e.g. Dirac delta
\delta
,
Heaviside function
H
, but also powers
\delta^{k}
,
k\in\mathbb{N}
,
and compositions
\delta\circ\delta
,
\delta^{k}\circ H^{h}
,
H^{h}\circ\delta^{k}
,
etc., for
h
,
k\in\mathbb{N}
.

Conversely, GRAF preserve a lot of classical results: they can be
thought of as infinitely long polynomials
f(x)=\mathchoice{\mathchoice{{\vphantom{\sum}}{}^{\scalebox{0.6}{$\raisebox{-1.59442pt}{$\rho\hskip-5.49028pt$}$}}}{{\vphantom{\sum}}{}^{\scalebox{0.56}{$\raisebox{-1.59442pt}{$\rho\hskip-5.49028pt$}$}}}{{\vphantom{\sum}}{}^{\scalebox{0.47}{$\raisebox{-1.59442pt}{$\rho\hskip-5.49028pt$}$}}}{{\vphantom{\sum}}{}^{\scalebox{0.35}{$\raisebox{-1.59442pt}{$\rho\hskip-5.49028pt$}$}}}\sum_{n\in\mathchoice{{\vphantom{\mathbb{N}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.6}{$\sigma$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{N}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.56}{$\sigma$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{N}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.47}{$\sigma$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{N}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.35}{$\sigma$}}}\widetilde{\mathbb{N}}\,}}{\mathchoice{{\vphantom{\sum}}{}^{\scalebox{0.6}{$\rho\hskip-2.04861pt$}}}{{\vphantom{\sum}}{}^{\scalebox{0.56}{$\rho\hskip-2.04861pt$}}}{{\vphantom{\sum}}{}^{\scalebox{0.47}{$\rho\hskip-2.04861pt$}}}{{\vphantom{\sum}}{}^{\scalebox{0.35}{$\rho\hskip-2.04861pt$}}}\sum_{n\in\mathchoice{{\vphantom{\mathbb{N}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.6}{$\sigma$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{N}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.56}{$\sigma$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{N}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.47}{$\sigma$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{N}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.35}{$\sigma$}}}\widetilde{\mathbb{N}}\,}}{\mathchoice{{\vphantom{\sum}}{}^{\scalebox{0.6}{$\rho\hskip-2.04861pt$}}}{{\vphantom{\sum}}{}^{\scalebox{0.56}{$\rho\hskip-2.04861pt$}}}{{\vphantom{\sum}}{}^{\scalebox{0.47}{$\rho\hskip-2.04861pt$}}}{{\vphantom{\sum}}{}^{\scalebox{0.35}{$\rho\hskip-2.04861pt$}}}\sum_{n\in\mathchoice{{\vphantom{\mathbb{N}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.6}{$\sigma$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{N}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.56}{$\sigma$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{N}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.47}{$\sigma$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{N}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.35}{$\sigma$}}}\widetilde{\mathbb{N}}\,}}{\mathchoice{{\vphantom{\sum}}{}^{\scalebox{0.6}{$\rho\hskip-2.04861pt$}}}{{\vphantom{\sum}}{}^{\scalebox{0.56}{$\rho\hskip-2.04861pt$}}}{{\vphantom{\sum}}{}^{\scalebox{0.47}{$\rho\hskip-2.04861pt$}}}{{\vphantom{\sum}}{}^{\scalebox{0.35}{$\rho\hskip-2.04861pt$}}}\sum_{n\in\mathchoice{{\vphantom{\mathbb{N}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.6}{$\sigma$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{N}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.56}{$\sigma$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{N}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.47}{$\sigma$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{N}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.35}{$\sigma$}}}\widetilde{\mathbb{N}}\,}}a_{n}(x-c)^{n}
,
with uniquely determined coefficient
a_{n}=\frac{f^{(n)}(c)}{n!}
,
they can be added, multiplied, composed, differentiated, integrated
term by term, are closed with respect to inverse function, etc. This
lays the foundation for a potential interesting generalization of
the Cauchy-Kowalevski theorem which is able to include many non-analytic
(but generalized real analytic) generalized functions.

Concerning the theory of analytic Colombeau generalized functions,
as developed in
[
26
]
for the real case and in
[
5
,
6
,
1
,
7
,
24
,
20
,
30
]

for the complex one, it is worth to mention that several properties
have been proved in both cases: closure with respect to composition,
integration over homotopic paths, Cauchy integral theorem, existence
of analytic representatives, identity theorem on a set of positive
Lebesgue measure, etc. (cf.
[
26
,
30
]
and references
therein). On the other hand, even if in
[
30
]
it is also proved
that each complex analytic Colombeau generalized functions can be
written as a Taylor series, necessarily this result holds only in
an infinitesimal neighborhood of each point. The impossibility to
extend this property to a finite neighborhood is a general drawback
of the use of ordinary series in a (Cauchy complete) non-Archimedean
framework instead of hyperseries, as explained in details in
[
29
]
.

We refer to
[
23
]
for basic notions such as the ring of Robinson-Colombeau,
subpoints, hypernatural numbers, supremum, infimum and hyperlimits,
and
[
29
]
for the notion of hyperseries as well as their notations
and properties. Once again, the ideas presented in the present article
can be useful to explore similar ideas in other non-Archimedean settings,
such as
[
3
,
17
,
18
,
4
,
19
,
21
,
28
]
.

2.
hyper-power series and its basic properties

2.1.
Definition of hyper-power series

In the entire paper,
\rho
and
\sigma
are two arbitrary gauges;
only when it will be needed, we will assume a relation between them,
such as
\sigma\leq\rho^{*}
or
\sigma\geq\rho^{*}
(see
[
29
]
).

A power series of
real numbers
is simply “a series of the
form
\sum_{n\in\mathbb{N}}a_{n}(x-c)^{n}
, where
x
,
c
,
a_{n}\in\mathbb{R}

for all
n\in\mathbb{N}
”. Actually, this (informal) definition allows
us to consider only finite sums
\sum_{n=0}^{N}a_{n}(x-c)^{n}
,
N\in\mathbb{N}
,
and hence to evaluate whether convergence holds or not. A similar
approach can be used for hyper-power series (HPS) if we think at the

\mathchoice{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.6}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.56}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.47}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.35}{$\rho$}}}\widetilde{\mathbb{R}}
-module
\mathchoice{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.6}{$\rho$}}_{\scalebox{0.6}{$\sigma$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.56}{$\rho$}}_{\scalebox{0.56}{$\sigma$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.47}{$\rho$}}_{\scalebox{0.47}{$\sigma$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.35}{$\rho$}}_{\scalebox{0.35}{$\sigma$}}}\widetilde{\mathbb{R}}_{\text{\rm s}}
of sequences for hyperseries exactly as
the space where we can consider hyperfinite sums regardless of convergence.
This is the idea to define the space
\mathchoice{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.6}{$\rho$}}_{\scalebox{0.6}{$\sigma$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.56}{$\rho$}}_{\scalebox{0.56}{$\sigma$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.47}{$\rho$}}_{\scalebox{0.47}{$\sigma$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.35}{$\rho$}}_{\scalebox{0.35}{$\sigma$}}}\widetilde{\mathbb{R}}\llbracket x-c\rrbracket
of formal HPS:

Definition 1
.

Let
x
,
c\in\mathchoice{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.6}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.56}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.47}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.35}{$\rho$}}}\widetilde{\mathbb{R}}
. We say
(b_{n})_{n}\in\mathchoice{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.6}{$\rho$}}_{\scalebox{0.6}{$\sigma$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.56}{$\rho$}}_{\scalebox{0.56}{$\sigma$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.47}{$\rho$}}_{\scalebox{0.47}{$\sigma$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.35}{$\rho$}}_{\scalebox{0.35}{$\sigma$}}}\widetilde{\mathbb{R}}\llbracket x-c\rrbracket

if and only if there exist
\left(a_{n\varepsilon}\right)_{n,\varepsilon}\in\mathbb{R}^{\mathbb{N}\times I}

and representatives
[x_{\varepsilon}]=x
,
[c_{\varepsilon}]=c
such that

(b_{n})_{n}=\left[a_{n\varepsilon}\cdot\left(x_{\varepsilon}-c_{\varepsilon}\right)^{n}\right]_{\text{{\rm s}}}\in\mathchoice{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.6}{$\rho$}}_{\scalebox{0.6}{$\sigma$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.56}{$\rho$}}_{\scalebox{0.56}{$\sigma$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.47}{$\rho$}}_{\scalebox{0.47}{$\sigma$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.35}{$\rho$}}_{\scalebox{0.35}{$\sigma$}}}\widetilde{\mathbb{R}}_{\text{\rm s}}.

(2.1)

Elements of
\mathchoice{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.6}{$\rho$}}_{\scalebox{0.6}{$\sigma$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.56}{$\rho$}}_{\scalebox{0.56}{$\sigma$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.47}{$\rho$}}_{\scalebox{0.47}{$\sigma$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.35}{$\rho$}}_{\scalebox{0.35}{$\sigma$}}}\widetilde{\mathbb{R}}\llbracket x-c\rrbracket
are called
formal
HPS because
here we are not considering their convergence. In other words, a formal
HPS is a hyper series (i.e. an equivalence class
(b_{n})_{n}\in\mathchoice{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.6}{$\rho$}}_{\scalebox{0.6}{$\sigma$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.56}{$\rho$}}_{\scalebox{0.56}{$\sigma$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.47}{$\rho$}}_{\scalebox{0.47}{$\sigma$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.35}{$\rho$}}_{\scalebox{0.35}{$\sigma$}}}\widetilde{\mathbb{R}}_{\text{\rm s}}

in the space of sequences for hyperseries) of the form
\left[a_{n\varepsilon}\cdot\left(x_{\varepsilon}-c_{\varepsilon}\right)^{n}\right]_{\text{{\rm s}}}
.

Remark 2
.

(i)

We explicitly note that
x-c
is not an indeterminate, like in the
case of formal power series
\mathbb{R}\llbracket x\rrbracket
, but a generalized
number of
\mathchoice{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.6}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.56}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.47}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.35}{$\rho$}}}\widetilde{\mathbb{R}}
. For example, in Lem.
10

below, we will prove that if
x-c=y-d
, then
\mathchoice{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.6}{$\rho$}}_{\scalebox{0.6}{$\sigma$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.56}{$\rho$}}_{\scalebox{0.56}{$\sigma$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.47}{$\rho$}}_{\scalebox{0.47}{$\sigma$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.35}{$\rho$}}_{\scalebox{0.35}{$\sigma$}}}\widetilde{\mathbb{R}}\llbracket x-c\rrbracket=\mathchoice{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.6}{$\rho$}}_{\scalebox{0.6}{$\sigma$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.56}{$\rho$}}_{\scalebox{0.56}{$\sigma$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.47}{$\rho$}}_{\scalebox{0.47}{$\sigma$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.35}{$\rho$}}_{\scalebox{0.35}{$\sigma$}}}\widetilde{\mathbb{R}}\llbracket y-d\rrbracket
.

(ii)

On the contrary with respect to the case of real numbers, being a
formal HPS, i.e. an element of
\mathchoice{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.6}{$\rho$}}_{\scalebox{0.6}{$\sigma$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.56}{$\rho$}}_{\scalebox{0.56}{$\sigma$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.47}{$\rho$}}_{\scalebox{0.47}{$\sigma$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.35}{$\rho$}}_{\scalebox{0.35}{$\sigma$}}}\widetilde{\mathbb{R}}\llbracket x-c\rrbracket
, depends on the interplay
of the two gauges
\rho
and
\sigma
: take e.g.
a_{n}=\frac{1}{n^{2}}

and
x-c=2
, so that for all
N\in\mathchoice{{\vphantom{\mathbb{N}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.6}{$\sigma$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{N}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.56}{$\sigma$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{N}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.47}{$\sigma$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{N}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.35}{$\sigma$}}}\widetilde{\mathbb{N}}
we have
\sum_{n=1}^{N}a_{n}(x-c)^{n}\geq\sum_{n=0}^{N}\frac{1}{n}\sim\log(N)
.
Therefore, taking e.g.
\sigma_{\varepsilon}=\exp\left(-\exp\left(\frac{1}{\rho_{\varepsilon}}\right)\right)

and
N_{\varepsilon}:=\text{int}\left(\sigma_{\varepsilon}\right)
, we have that

\left(\log N_{\varepsilon}\right)\notin\mathbb{R}_{\rho}
and hence we cannot even
consider hyperfinite sums of this form. Informally stated, for this
gauge
\sigma
, we have that
\mathchoice{\mathchoice{{\vphantom{\sum}}{}^{\scalebox{0.6}{$\raisebox{-1.59442pt}{$\rho\hskip-5.49028pt$}$}}}{{\vphantom{\sum}}{}^{\scalebox{0.56}{$\raisebox{-1.59442pt}{$\rho\hskip-5.49028pt$}$}}}{{\vphantom{\sum}}{}^{\scalebox{0.47}{$\raisebox{-1.59442pt}{$\rho\hskip-5.49028pt$}$}}}{{\vphantom{\sum}}{}^{\scalebox{0.35}{$\raisebox{-1.59442pt}{$\rho\hskip-5.49028pt$}$}}}\sum_{n\in\mathchoice{{\vphantom{\mathbb{N}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.6}{$\sigma$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{N}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.56}{$\sigma$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{N}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.47}{$\sigma$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{N}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.35}{$\sigma$}}}\widetilde{\mathbb{N}}\,}}{\mathchoice{{\vphantom{\sum}}{}^{\scalebox{0.6}{$\rho\hskip-2.04861pt$}}}{{\vphantom{\sum}}{}^{\scalebox{0.56}{$\rho\hskip-2.04861pt$}}}{{\vphantom{\sum}}{}^{\scalebox{0.47}{$\rho\hskip-2.04861pt$}}}{{\vphantom{\sum}}{}^{\scalebox{0.35}{$\rho\hskip-2.04861pt$}}}\sum_{n\in\mathchoice{{\vphantom{\mathbb{N}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.6}{$\sigma$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{N}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.56}{$\sigma$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{N}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.47}{$\sigma$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{N}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.35}{$\sigma$}}}\widetilde{\mathbb{N}}\,}}{\mathchoice{{\vphantom{\sum}}{}^{\scalebox{0.6}{$\rho\hskip-2.04861pt$}}}{{\vphantom{\sum}}{}^{\scalebox{0.56}{$\rho\hskip-2.04861pt$}}}{{\vphantom{\sum}}{}^{\scalebox{0.47}{$\rho\hskip-2.04861pt$}}}{{\vphantom{\sum}}{}^{\scalebox{0.35}{$\rho\hskip-2.04861pt$}}}\sum_{n\in\mathchoice{{\vphantom{\mathbb{N}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.6}{$\sigma$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{N}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.56}{$\sigma$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{N}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.47}{$\sigma$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{N}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.35}{$\sigma$}}}\widetilde{\mathbb{N}}\,}}{\mathchoice{{\vphantom{\sum}}{}^{\scalebox{0.6}{$\rho\hskip-2.04861pt$}}}{{\vphantom{\sum}}{}^{\scalebox{0.56}{$\rho\hskip-2.04861pt$}}}{{\vphantom{\sum}}{}^{\scalebox{0.47}{$\rho\hskip-2.04861pt$}}}{{\vphantom{\sum}}{}^{\scalebox{0.35}{$\rho\hskip-2.04861pt$}}}\sum_{n\in\mathchoice{{\vphantom{\mathbb{N}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.6}{$\sigma$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{N}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.56}{$\sigma$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{N}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.47}{$\sigma$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{N}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.35}{$\sigma$}}}\widetilde{\mathbb{N}}\,}}\frac{1}{n^{2}}2^{n}

is
not
a formal HPS, i.e. even before considering its convergence
or not, we cannot compute
\sigma
-hyperfinite sums and get a number
in
\mathchoice{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.6}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.56}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.47}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.35}{$\rho$}}}\widetilde{\mathbb{R}}
.

(iii)

In
[
29
]
, we proved that if
x
is finite, then
\left[\frac{x^{n}}{n!}\right]_{\text{{\rm s}}}\in\mathchoice{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.6}{$\rho$}}_{\scalebox{0.6}{$\sigma$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.56}{$\rho$}}_{\scalebox{0.56}{$\sigma$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.47}{$\rho$}}_{\scalebox{0.47}{$\sigma$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.35}{$\rho$}}_{\scalebox{0.35}{$\sigma$}}}\widetilde{\mathbb{R}}\llbracket x\rrbracket

is a formal HPS for all gauges
\rho
,
\sigma
. In Sec.
14
,
we will prove that
\left[\frac{\left(\mathchoice{\hbox{\rm d}\rho}{\hbox{\rm d}\rho}{\scalebox{0.75}{$\hbox{\rm d}\rho$}}{\scalebox{0.35}{$\hbox{\rm d}\rho$}}^{-1}\right)^{n}}{n!}\right]_{\text{{\rm s}}}\notin\mathchoice{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.6}{$\rho$}}_{\scalebox{0.6}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.56}{$\rho$}}_{\scalebox{0.56}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.47}{$\rho$}}_{\scalebox{0.47}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.35}{$\rho$}}_{\scalebox{0.35}{$\rho$}}}\widetilde{\mathbb{R}}\llbracket\mathchoice{\hbox{\rm d}\rho}{\hbox{\rm d}\rho}{\scalebox{0.75}{$\hbox{\rm d}\rho$}}{\scalebox{0.35}{$\hbox{\rm d}\rho$}}^{-1}\rrbracket
;
on the other hand, we will also see that if
x\leq\log\left(\mathchoice{\hbox{\rm d}\rho}{\hbox{\rm d}\rho}{\scalebox{0.75}{$\hbox{\rm d}\rho$}}{\scalebox{0.35}{$\hbox{\rm d}\rho$}}^{-N}\right)

and
\mathchoice{\hbox{\rm d}\sigma}{\hbox{\rm d}\sigma}{\scalebox{0.75}{$\hbox{\rm d}\sigma$}}{\scalebox{0.35}{$\hbox{\rm d}\sigma$}}^{Q}\leq\mathchoice{\hbox{\rm d}\rho}{\hbox{\rm d}\rho}{\scalebox{0.75}{$\hbox{\rm d}\rho$}}{\scalebox{0.35}{$\hbox{\rm d}\rho$}}^{N}
for some
Q\in\mathbb{N}
, then
\left[\frac{x^{n}}{n!}\right]_{\text{{\rm s}}}\in\mathchoice{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.6}{$\rho$}}_{\scalebox{0.6}{$\sigma$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.56}{$\rho$}}_{\scalebox{0.56}{$\sigma$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.47}{$\rho$}}_{\scalebox{0.47}{$\sigma$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.35}{$\rho$}}_{\scalebox{0.35}{$\sigma$}}}\widetilde{\mathbb{R}}\llbracket x\rrbracket

is a formal HPS.

The previous Def.
1
sets immediate problems concerning
independence of representatives: every time we start from
\left[a_{n\varepsilon}\right]\in\mathchoice{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.6}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.56}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.47}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.35}{$\rho$}}}\widetilde{\mathbb{R}}
,
for all
n\in\mathbb{N}
,
[x_{\varepsilon}]=x
,
[c_{\varepsilon}]=c
and we have that

\left[a_{n\varepsilon}\cdot\left(x_{\varepsilon}-c_{\varepsilon}\right)^{n}\right]_{\text{{\rm s}}}\in\mathchoice{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.6}{$\rho$}}_{\scalebox{0.6}{$\sigma$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.56}{$\rho$}}_{\scalebox{0.56}{$\sigma$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.47}{$\rho$}}_{\scalebox{0.47}{$\sigma$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.35}{$\rho$}}_{\scalebox{0.35}{$\sigma$}}}\widetilde{\mathbb{R}}_{\text{\rm s}}
,
we can consider whether the corresponding formal HPS
\mathchoice{\mathchoice{{\vphantom{\sum}}{}^{\scalebox{0.6}{$\raisebox{-1.59442pt}{$\rho\hskip-5.49028pt$}$}}}{{\vphantom{\sum}}{}^{\scalebox{0.56}{$\raisebox{-1.59442pt}{$\rho\hskip-5.49028pt$}$}}}{{\vphantom{\sum}}{}^{\scalebox{0.47}{$\raisebox{-1.59442pt}{$\rho\hskip-5.49028pt$}$}}}{{\vphantom{\sum}}{}^{\scalebox{0.35}{$\raisebox{-1.59442pt}{$\rho\hskip-5.49028pt$}$}}}\sum_{n\in\mathchoice{{\vphantom{\mathbb{N}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.6}{$\sigma$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{N}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.56}{$\sigma$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{N}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.47}{$\sigma$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{N}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.35}{$\sigma$}}}\widetilde{\mathbb{N}}\,}}{\mathchoice{{\vphantom{\sum}}{}^{\scalebox{0.6}{$\rho\hskip-2.04861pt$}}}{{\vphantom{\sum}}{}^{\scalebox{0.56}{$\rho\hskip-2.04861pt$}}}{{\vphantom{\sum}}{}^{\scalebox{0.47}{$\rho\hskip-2.04861pt$}}}{{\vphantom{\sum}}{}^{\scalebox{0.35}{$\rho\hskip-2.04861pt$}}}\sum_{n\in\mathchoice{{\vphantom{\mathbb{N}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.6}{$\sigma$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{N}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.56}{$\sigma$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{N}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.47}{$\sigma$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{N}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.35}{$\sigma$}}}\widetilde{\mathbb{N}}\,}}{\mathchoice{{\vphantom{\sum}}{}^{\scalebox{0.6}{$\rho\hskip-2.04861pt$}}}{{\vphantom{\sum}}{}^{\scalebox{0.56}{$\rho\hskip-2.04861pt$}}}{{\vphantom{\sum}}{}^{\scalebox{0.47}{$\rho\hskip-2.04861pt$}}}{{\vphantom{\sum}}{}^{\scalebox{0.35}{$\rho\hskip-2.04861pt$}}}\sum_{n\in\mathchoice{{\vphantom{\mathbb{N}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.6}{$\sigma$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{N}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.56}{$\sigma$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{N}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.47}{$\sigma$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{N}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.35}{$\sigma$}}}\widetilde{\mathbb{N}}\,}}{\mathchoice{{\vphantom{\sum}}{}^{\scalebox{0.6}{$\rho\hskip-2.04861pt$}}}{{\vphantom{\sum}}{}^{\scalebox{0.56}{$\rho\hskip-2.04861pt$}}}{{\vphantom{\sum}}{}^{\scalebox{0.47}{$\rho\hskip-2.04861pt$}}}{{\vphantom{\sum}}{}^{\scalebox{0.35}{$\rho\hskip-2.04861pt$}}}\sum_{n\in\mathchoice{{\vphantom{\mathbb{N}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.6}{$\sigma$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{N}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.56}{$\sigma$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{N}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.47}{$\sigma$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{N}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.35}{$\sigma$}}}\widetilde{\mathbb{N}}\,}}[a_{n\varepsilon}]\cdot\left([x_{\varepsilon}]-[c_{\varepsilon}]\right)^{n}

converges or not. On the other hand, we also have to prove that it
is well-defined, i.e. that taking different representatives
\left[\bar{a}_{n\varepsilon}\right]=\left[a_{n\varepsilon}\right]
,

[\bar{x}_{\varepsilon}]=x
,
[\bar{c}_{\varepsilon}]=c
, we have
\left[a_{n\varepsilon}\cdot\left(x_{\varepsilon}-c_{\varepsilon}\right)^{n}\right]_{\text{{\rm s}}}=\left[\bar{a}_{n\varepsilon}\cdot\left(\bar{x}_{\varepsilon}-\bar{c}_{\varepsilon}\right)^{n}\right]_{\text{{\rm s}}}
.
However, from
[
29
, Sec. 2]
it follows that we can have
x-c=1

and
\left[a_{n\varepsilon}\right]=\left[\bar{a}_{n\varepsilon}\right]=0
for all

n\in\mathbb{N}
, but

\left[\sum_{n=0}^{N_{\varepsilon}}a_{n\varepsilon}(x_{\varepsilon}-c_{\varepsilon})^{n}\right]\neq\left[\sum_{n=0}^{N_{\varepsilon}}\bar{a}_{n\varepsilon}(x_{\varepsilon}-c_{\varepsilon})^{n}\right].

This means that
(b_{n})_{n}:=\left[a_{n\varepsilon}(x_{\varepsilon}-c_{\varepsilon})^{n}\right]_{\text{s}}

and
(\bar{b}_{n})_{n}:=\left[\bar{a}_{n\varepsilon}(x_{\varepsilon}-c_{\varepsilon})^{n}\right]_{\text{s}}

yield two different formal HPS (see
[
29
, Thm. 4]
) and hence,
in general, the operation

\left((a_{n\varepsilon})_{n,\varepsilon},(x_{\varepsilon}),(c_{\varepsilon})\right)\in\mathchoice{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.6}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.56}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.47}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.35}{$\rho$}}}\widetilde{\mathbb{R}}^{\mathbb{N}}\times\mathchoice{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.6}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.56}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.47}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.35}{$\rho$}}}\widetilde{\mathbb{R}}^{2}\mapsto(b_{n})_{n}:=\left[a_{n\varepsilon}(x_{\varepsilon}-c_{\varepsilon})^{n}\right]_{\text{s}}\in\mathchoice{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.6}{$\rho$}}_{\scalebox{0.6}{$\sigma$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.56}{$\rho$}}_{\scalebox{0.56}{$\sigma$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.47}{$\rho$}}_{\scalebox{0.47}{$\sigma$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.35}{$\rho$}}_{\scalebox{0.35}{$\sigma$}}}\widetilde{\mathbb{R}}_{\text{\rm s}}

is not well-defined. The problem can also be addressed differently:
what notion of equality do we have to set on a suitable subring of

\mathbb{R}^{\mathbb{N}\times I}
so as to have independence on representatives?
This notion of equality naturally emerges in proving that the following
definition of radius of convergence is well-defined (see Lem.
4
).
What subring we need to consider arises from the idea to include
\left(\frac{\delta_{\varepsilon}^{(n)}(0)}{n!}\right)_{n,\varepsilon}
in
it, where
\delta=\left[\delta_{\varepsilon}(-)\right]
is a suitable embedding
of Dirac’s delta function (see Example
5
.
(v)
).

2.2.
Radius of convergence

The idea to define the radius of convergence corresponding to coefficients

(a_{n\varepsilon})_{n,\varepsilon}\in\mathbb{R}^{\mathbb{N}\times I}
is that it does not matter
if

\left(\limsup_{n\to+\infty}\left|a_{n\varepsilon}\right|^{1/n}\right)^{-1}\in\mathbb{R}\cup\{+\infty\}

yields a non
\rho
-moderate net (for example for
\varepsilon\in L\subseteq_{0}I
)
because this case would intuitively identify a radius of convergence
larger than any infinite number in
\mathchoice{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.6}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.56}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.47}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.35}{$\rho$}}}\widetilde{\mathbb{R}}
:

Definition 3
.

(i)

Let
\overline{\mathbb{R}}:=\mathbb{R}\cup\{-\infty,\infty\}
be
the extended real number system with the usual (partially defined)
operations. We set
\mathchoice{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.6}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.56}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.47}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.35}{$\rho$}}}\overline{\mathbb{R}}:=\overline{\mathbb{R}}^{I}/\sim_{\rho}
, where
for arbitrary
(x_{\varepsilon})
,
(y_{\varepsilon})\in\overline{\mathbb{R}}^{I}
, as
usual we define

(x_{\varepsilon})\sim_{\rho}(y_{\varepsilon})\quad:\iff\quad\forall q\in\mathbb{N}\,\forall^{0}\varepsilon:\ \left|x_{\varepsilon}-y_{\varepsilon}\right|\leq\rho_{\varepsilon}^{q}.

In
\mathchoice{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.6}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.56}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.47}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.35}{$\rho$}}}\overline{\mathbb{R}}
, we can also consider the standard order relation

x\leq y\quad:\iff\quad\exists[x_{\varepsilon}]=x,[y_{\varepsilon}]=y\,\forall^{0}\varepsilon:\ x_{\varepsilon}\leq y_{\varepsilon}.

Note that
\left(\mathchoice{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.6}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.56}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.47}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.35}{$\rho$}}}\overline{\mathbb{R}}\setminus\{-\infty\},+,\leq\right)
is an
ordered group but, since we are considering arbitrary nets
\overline{\mathbb{R}}^{I}
,
the set
\mathchoice{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.6}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.56}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.47}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.35}{$\rho$}}}\overline{\mathbb{R}}
is not a ring: e.g.
+\infty\cdot 0
is still
undefined and
+\infty\cdot[z_{\varepsilon}]=[+\infty]
for all
(z_{\varepsilon})\in\mathbb{R}_{>0}^{I}
.

(ii)

Moreover, we denote by
\mathchoice{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.6}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.56}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.47}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.35}{$\rho$}}}\widetilde{\mathbb{R}}_{\text{\rm c}}:=\left(\mathbb{R}^{\mathbb{N}\times I}\right)_{\rho}/\simeq_{\rho}

the quotient ring of
coefficients for HPS
, where

\left(a_{n\varepsilon}\right)_{n,\varepsilon}\in\left(\mathbb{R}^{\mathbb{N}\times I}\right)_{\rho}:\!\iff\exists Q,R\in\mathbb{N}\,\forall^{0}\varepsilon\,\forall n\in\mathbb{N}:\ \left|a_{n\varepsilon}\right|\leq\rho_{\varepsilon}^{-nQ-R}

(2.2)

is the ring of
weakly
\rho
-moderate nets
, and

\left(a_{n\varepsilon}\right)_{n,\varepsilon}\simeq_{\rho}\left(\bar{a}_{n\varepsilon}\right)_{n,\varepsilon}:\!\iff\forall q,r\in\mathbb{N}\,\forall^{0}\varepsilon\,\forall n\in\mathbb{N}:\ \left|a_{n\varepsilon}-\bar{a}_{n\varepsilon}\right|\leq\rho_{\varepsilon}^{nq+r}

(2.3)

(in this case, we say that these two nets are
strongly
\rho
-equivalent
).
Equivalence classes of
\mathchoice{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.6}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.56}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.47}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.35}{$\rho$}}}\widetilde{\mathbb{R}}_{\text{\rm c}}
are denoted by
(a_{n})_{{\rm c}}:=\left[a_{n\varepsilon}\right]_{\text{c}}\in\mathchoice{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.6}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.56}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.47}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.35}{$\rho$}}}\widetilde{\mathbb{R}}_{\text{\rm c}}
.

(iii)

Finally, if
(a_{n})_{{\rm c}}=\left[a_{n\varepsilon}\right]_{\text{c}}\in\mathchoice{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.6}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.56}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.47}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.35}{$\rho$}}}\widetilde{\mathbb{R}}_{\text{\rm c}}
,
then we set
\text{\rm rad}\left(a_{n}\right)_{{\rm c}{\varepsilon}}:=r_{\varepsilon}.
and
\text{\rm rad}\left(a_{n}\right)_{\rm c}=:[r_{\varepsilon}]\in\mathchoice{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.6}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.56}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.47}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.35}{$\rho$}}}\overline{\mathbb{R}}
,
where

r_{\varepsilon}:=\left(\limsup_{n\to+\infty}\left|a_{n\varepsilon}\right|^{1/n}\right)^{-1}\in\mathbb{R}\cup\{+\infty\}.

(2.4)

In the following lemma, we prove that
\text{\rm rad}\left(a_{n}\right)_{\rm c}

is well-defined:

Lemma 4
.

Let
(a_{n})_{{\rm c}}=\left[a_{n\varepsilon}\right]_{\text{{\rm c}}}=\left[\bar{a}_{n\varepsilon}\right]_{\text{{\rm c}}}\in\mathchoice{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.6}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.56}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.47}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.35}{$\rho$}}}\widetilde{\mathbb{R}}_{\text{\rm c}}
.
Define
r_{\varepsilon}
as in (
2.4
) and similarly define
\bar{r}_{\varepsilon}

using
\bar{a}_{n\varepsilon}
. Then
(r_{\varepsilon})\sim_{\rho}(\bar{r}_{\varepsilon})
,
and hence
[r_{\varepsilon}]=[\bar{r}_{\varepsilon}]
in
\mathchoice{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.6}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.56}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.47}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.35}{$\rho$}}}\overline{\mathbb{R}}
.

Proof.

For all
\varepsilon\in I
and all
n\in\mathbb{N}_{>0}
, we have
\left|\bar{a}_{n\varepsilon}\right|^{1/n}\leq\left(\left|\bar{a}_{n\varepsilon}-a_{n\varepsilon}\right|+\left|a_{n\varepsilon}\right|\right)^{1/n}
.
The binomial formula yields
(x+y)\leq\left(x^{1/n}+y^{1/n}\right)^{n}

for all
x
,
y\in\mathbb{R}_{\geq 0}
, so that
\left|\bar{a}_{n\varepsilon}\right|^{1/n}\leq\left|\bar{a}_{n\varepsilon}-a_{n\varepsilon}\right|^{1/n}+\left|a_{n\varepsilon}\right|^{1/n}
.
Setting
r=0
in (
2.3
), for all
q\in\mathbb{N}
and for

\varepsilon
small we have

\forall n\in\mathbb{N}:\ \left|a_{n\varepsilon}-\bar{a}_{n\varepsilon}\right|\leq\rho_{\varepsilon}^{nq}.

Therefore, for the same
\varepsilon
we get
\left|\bar{a}_{n\varepsilon}\right|^{1/n}\leq\rho_{\varepsilon}^{q}+\left|a_{n\varepsilon}\right|^{1/n}
.
Taking the limit superior we obtain
\limsup_{n\to+\infty}\left|\bar{a}_{n\varepsilon}\right|^{1/n}\leq\rho_{\varepsilon}^{q}+\limsup_{n\to+\infty}\left|a_{n\varepsilon}\right|^{1/n}
.
Inverting the role of
(a_{n\varepsilon})_{n,\varepsilon}
and
(\bar{a}_{n\varepsilon})_{n,\varepsilon}

we finally obtain

\forall^{0}\varepsilon:\ -\rho_{\varepsilon}^{q}\leq\limsup_{n\to+\infty}\left|a_{n\varepsilon}\right|^{1/n}-\limsup_{n\to+\infty}\left|\bar{a}_{n\varepsilon}\right|^{1/n}\leq\rho_{\varepsilon}^{q},

which proves the claim.
∎

Remark 5
.

(i)

If
(a_{n})_{{\rm c}}=\left[a_{n\varepsilon}\right]_{\text{{\rm c}}}\in\mathchoice{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.6}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.56}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.47}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.35}{$\rho$}}}\widetilde{\mathbb{R}}_{\text{\rm c}}
,
then for each fixed
n\in\mathbb{N}
, we have that
[\left(a_{n\varepsilon}\right)_{\varepsilon}]\in\mathchoice{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.6}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.56}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.47}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.35}{$\rho$}}}\widetilde{\mathbb{R}}
,
i.e. the net
\left(a_{n\varepsilon}\right)_{\varepsilon}
is
\rho
-moderate.
This is the main motivation to consider the exponent “
-R
” in
(
2.2
) (recall that in our notation
0\in\mathbb{N}
): without
the term “
-R
”, the only possibility to have
(a_{n})_{c}\in\mathchoice{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.6}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.56}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.47}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.35}{$\rho$}}}\overline{\mathbb{R}}

is that
|a_{0}|\leq 1
, which is an unnecessary limitation. Similarly,
we can motivate why we are considering the quantifier “
\forall n\in\mathbb{N}
”
in the same formula (instead of, e.g., “
\exists N\in\mathbb{N}\,\forall n\in\mathbb{N}_{\geq N}
”).
The proof of the next Lem.
10
will motivate why
in (
2.2
) we consider the uniform property “
\forall^{0}\varepsilon\,\forall n\in\mathbb{N}
”
and not “
\forall n\in\mathbb{N}\,\forall^{0}\varepsilon
”.

(ii)

Note that
\mathchoice{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.6}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.56}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.47}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.35}{$\rho$}}}\widetilde{\mathbb{R}}\subseteq\mathchoice{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.6}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.56}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.47}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.35}{$\rho$}}}\overline{\mathbb{R}}

because the notion of equality
\sim_{\rho}
in the two quotient
sets is the same and because if
(x_{\varepsilon})
is
\rho
-moderate
and
(x_{\varepsilon})\sim_{\rho}(y_{\varepsilon})
, then also
(y_{\varepsilon})
is

\rho
-moderate.

(iii)

Condition (
2.2
) of being weakly
\rho
-moderate represents
a constrain on what coefficients
a_{n}
we can consider in a hyperseries.
For example, if
(a_{n})_{n\in\mathbb{N}}
is a sequence of real numbers
satisfying
|a_{n}|\leq p(n)
, where
p\in\mathbb{R}[x]
is a polynomial,
then
p(n)\leq\rho_{\varepsilon}^{-nQ}
for all
\varepsilon
sufficiently small
and for all
n\in\mathbb{N}
if
Q\geq\max\left(1,\max\left\{-\frac{\log n}{p(n)\log\rho_{\varepsilon}}\mid n<N_{1}\right\}\right)
,
where
\frac{\log n}{P(n)}\leq 1
for all
n\geq N_{1}
and
-\frac{1}{\log\rho_{\varepsilon}}\leq 1
.
Hence
\left(a_{n}\right)_{n,\varepsilon}\in\left(\mathbb{R}^{\mathbb{N}\times I}\right)_{\rho}

is weakly
\rho
-moderate. On the contrary, we cannot have
n^{n}\leq\rho_{\varepsilon}^{-nQ-R}=\rho_{\varepsilon}^{-R}\left(\frac{1}{\rho_{\varepsilon}^{Q}}\right)^{n}

for all
n\in\mathbb{N}
. Similarly
(n!)_{n\in\mathbb{N}}
is not weakly
\rho
-moderate
and hence our theory does not apply to a “hyperseries” of the
form
\mathchoice{\mathchoice{{\vphantom{\sum}}{}^{\scalebox{0.6}{$\raisebox{-1.59442pt}{$\rho\hskip-5.49028pt$}$}}}{{\vphantom{\sum}}{}^{\scalebox{0.56}{$\raisebox{-1.59442pt}{$\rho\hskip-5.49028pt$}$}}}{{\vphantom{\sum}}{}^{\scalebox{0.47}{$\raisebox{-1.59442pt}{$\rho\hskip-5.49028pt$}$}}}{{\vphantom{\sum}}{}^{\scalebox{0.35}{$\raisebox{-1.59442pt}{$\rho\hskip-5.49028pt$}$}}}\sum_{n\in\mathchoice{{\vphantom{\mathbb{N}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.6}{$\sigma$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{N}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.56}{$\sigma$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{N}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.47}{$\sigma$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{N}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.35}{$\sigma$}}}\widetilde{\mathbb{N}}\,}}{\mathchoice{{\vphantom{\sum}}{}^{\scalebox{0.6}{$\rho\hskip-2.04861pt$}}}{{\vphantom{\sum}}{}^{\scalebox{0.56}{$\rho\hskip-2.04861pt$}}}{{\vphantom{\sum}}{}^{\scalebox{0.47}{$\rho\hskip-2.04861pt$}}}{{\vphantom{\sum}}{}^{\scalebox{0.35}{$\rho\hskip-2.04861pt$}}}\sum_{n\in\mathchoice{{\vphantom{\mathbb{N}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.6}{$\sigma$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{N}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.56}{$\sigma$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{N}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.47}{$\sigma$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{N}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.35}{$\sigma$}}}\widetilde{\mathbb{N}}\,}}{\mathchoice{{\vphantom{\sum}}{}^{\scalebox{0.6}{$\rho\hskip-2.04861pt$}}}{{\vphantom{\sum}}{}^{\scalebox{0.56}{$\rho\hskip-2.04861pt$}}}{{\vphantom{\sum}}{}^{\scalebox{0.47}{$\rho\hskip-2.04861pt$}}}{{\vphantom{\sum}}{}^{\scalebox{0.35}{$\rho\hskip-2.04861pt$}}}\sum_{n\in\mathchoice{{\vphantom{\mathbb{N}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.6}{$\sigma$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{N}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.56}{$\sigma$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{N}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.47}{$\sigma$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{N}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.35}{$\sigma$}}}\widetilde{\mathbb{N}}\,}}{\mathchoice{{\vphantom{\sum}}{}^{\scalebox{0.6}{$\rho\hskip-2.04861pt$}}}{{\vphantom{\sum}}{}^{\scalebox{0.56}{$\rho\hskip-2.04861pt$}}}{{\vphantom{\sum}}{}^{\scalebox{0.47}{$\rho\hskip-2.04861pt$}}}{{\vphantom{\sum}}{}^{\scalebox{0.35}{$\rho\hskip-2.04861pt$}}}\sum_{n\in\mathchoice{{\vphantom{\mathbb{N}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.6}{$\sigma$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{N}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.56}{$\sigma$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{N}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.47}{$\sigma$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{N}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.35}{$\sigma$}}}\widetilde{\mathbb{N}}\,}}n!\cdot x^{n}
. On the other hand, in
Lem.
7
.
(i)
we will show that,
as a consequence of considering only weakly moderate coefficients,
the radius of convergence of our hyperseries is always strictly positive.

(iv)

Let
a_{n\varepsilon}=\rho_{\varepsilon}^{\frac{n+1}{\varepsilon}}
,
so that
\left[a_{n\varepsilon}\right]_{\text{c}}=0
. The corresponding
radius of convergence is
r_{\varepsilon}=\lim_{n\to+\infty}|a_{n\varepsilon}|^{1/n}=\rho_{\varepsilon}^{1/\varepsilon}

which is not
\rho
-moderate. In general, if
\text{\rm rad}\left(a_{n}\right)_{\rm c}=[r_{\varepsilon}]=:r\in\mathchoice{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.6}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.56}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.47}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.35}{$\rho$}}}\overline{\mathbb{R}}
,
we can have different behavior on different subpoints, e.g.
r|_{L_{1}}=+\infty
,

r|_{L_{2}}\in\mathchoice{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.6}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.56}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.47}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.35}{$\rho$}}}\widetilde{\mathbb{R}}
,
r|_{L_{3}}
non
\rho
-moderate, etc., where

L_{i}\subseteq_{0}I
. This behavior is studied in Lem.
7

below.

(v)

Let
\mu:=\mathcal{F}^{-1}(\beta)\in\mathcal{S}(\mathbb{R})

be a Colombeau mollifier defined as the inverse Fourier transform
of a smooth, supported in
[-1,1]_{\mathbb{R}}
, even bump function
0\leq\beta\leq 1

which identically equals
1
in a neighborhood of
0
(see e.g.
[
15
]
).
Let
i_{\mathbb{R}}^{b}
be the embedding of Schwartz distributions into
generalized smooth functions (GSF) defined by
\mu
and by the infinite
number
b\in\mathchoice{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.6}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.56}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.47}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.35}{$\rho$}}}\widetilde{\mathbb{R}}
(see e.g.
[
13
]
). The Schwartz’s Paley-Wiener
theorem implies that
\mu
is an entire function and we know that
if
\mathchoice{\hbox{\rm d}\rho}{\hbox{\rm d}\rho}{\scalebox{0.75}{$\hbox{\rm d}\rho$}}{\scalebox{0.35}{$\hbox{\rm d}\rho$}}^{-Q}\geq b=[b_{\varepsilon}]\geq\mathchoice{\hbox{\rm d}\rho}{\hbox{\rm d}\rho}{\scalebox{0.75}{$\hbox{\rm d}\rho$}}{\scalebox{0.35}{$\hbox{\rm d}\rho$}}^{-R}
, for some
Q
,

R\in\mathbb{R}_{>0}
, then the embedding of Dirac delta
\delta:=\iota_{\mathbb{R}}^{b}\left(\varphi\mapsto\varphi(0)\right)\in\mathchoice{{\vphantom{\mathcal{G}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.6}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathcal{G}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.56}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathcal{G}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.47}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathcal{G}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.35}{$\rho$}}}\mathcal{GC}^{\infty}(\mathchoice{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.6}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.56}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.47}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.35}{$\rho$}}}\widetilde{\mathbb{R}},\mathchoice{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.6}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.56}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.47}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.35}{$\rho$}}}\widetilde{\mathbb{R}})

is defined by the net
\delta_{\varepsilon}(x)=b_{\varepsilon}\mu(b_{\varepsilon}x)
(see
e.g.
[
13
]
). For
n\in\mathbb{N}
, we have
\mu^{(n)}(0)=\frac{1}{2\pi}\int\beta(x)(ix)^{n}\,\mathchoice{\hbox{\rm d}x}{\hbox{\rm d}x}{\scalebox{0.75}{$\hbox{\rm d}x$}}{\scalebox{0.35}{$\hbox{\rm d}x$}}=0

if
n
is odd and
\left|\mu^{(n)}(0)\right|\leq\frac{1}{2\pi}\left[\frac{x^{n+1}}{n+1}\right]_{-1}^{1}\leq 1

if
n
if even. Thereby
\left|\frac{\delta_{\varepsilon}^{(n)}(0)}{n!}\right|=\left|\frac{\mu^{(n)}(0)}{n!}b_{\varepsilon}^{n+1}\right|\leq\frac{1}{n!}\rho_{\varepsilon}^{-nQ-Q}\leq\rho_{\varepsilon}^{-nQ-Q}
.
This inequality shows that
\left(\frac{\delta^{(n)}(0)}{n!}\right)_{\text{c}}\in\mathchoice{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.6}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.56}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.47}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.35}{$\rho$}}}\widetilde{\mathbb{R}}_{\text{\rm c}}

and motivates our definition of weakly
\rho
-moderate nets. The
corresponding radius of convergence is
r_{\varepsilon}^{-1}=\limsup_{n\to+\infty}\left|\frac{\delta_{\varepsilon}^{(n)}(0)}{n!}\right|^{1/n}=\limsup_{n\to+\infty}b_{\varepsilon}^{1+1/n}\left|\frac{\mu^{(n)}(0)}{n!}\right|^{1/n}=b_{\varepsilon}\cdot 0=0
,
i.e.
\text{\rm rad}\left(\frac{\delta^{(n)}(0)}{n!}\right)_{\rm c}=+\infty
.

(vi)

Let
(a_{n})_{{\rm c}}=[a_{n\varepsilon}]_{{\rm c}}\in\mathchoice{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.6}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.56}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.47}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.35}{$\rho$}}}\widetilde{\mathbb{R}}_{\text{\rm c}}
, and assume
that for all
\varepsilon
there exists
r_{\varepsilon}:=\left(\lim_{n\to+\infty}|a_{n\varepsilon}|^{1/n}\right)^{-1}

such that
r:=[r_{\varepsilon}]\in\mathchoice{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.6}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.56}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.47}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.35}{$\rho$}}}\widetilde{\mathbb{R}}
. Then from
[
23
, Thm. 28]
,
for some gauge
\sigma\leq\rho
we have
\mathchoice{\mathchoice{{\vphantom{\lim}}{}^{\scalebox{0.6}{$\raisebox{-0.39864pt}{$\rho\hskip-5.49028pt$}$}}}{{\vphantom{\lim}}{}^{\scalebox{0.56}{$\raisebox{-0.39864pt}{$\rho\hskip-5.49028pt$}$}}}{{\vphantom{\lim}}{}^{\scalebox{0.47}{$\raisebox{-0.39864pt}{$\rho\hskip-5.49028pt$}$}}}{{\vphantom{\lim}}{}^{\scalebox{0.35}{$\raisebox{-0.39864pt}{$\rho\hskip-5.49028pt$}$}}}\lim_{n\in\mathchoice{{\vphantom{\mathbb{N}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.6}{$\sigma$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{N}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.56}{$\sigma$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{N}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.47}{$\sigma$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{N}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.35}{$\sigma$}}}\widetilde{\mathbb{N}}\,}}{\mathchoice{{\vphantom{\lim}}{}^{\scalebox{0.6}{$\rho\hskip-2.04861pt$}}}{{\vphantom{\lim}}{}^{\scalebox{0.56}{$\rho\hskip-2.04861pt$}}}{{\vphantom{\lim}}{}^{\scalebox{0.47}{$\rho\hskip-2.04861pt$}}}{{\vphantom{\lim}}{}^{\scalebox{0.35}{$\rho\hskip-2.04861pt$}}}\lim_{n\in\mathchoice{{\vphantom{\mathbb{N}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.6}{$\sigma$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{N}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.56}{$\sigma$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{N}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.47}{$\sigma$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{N}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.35}{$\sigma$}}}\widetilde{\mathbb{N}}\,}}{\mathchoice{{\vphantom{\lim}}{}^{\scalebox{0.6}{$\rho\hskip-2.04861pt$}}}{{\vphantom{\lim}}{}^{\scalebox{0.56}{$\rho\hskip-2.04861pt$}}}{{\vphantom{\lim}}{}^{\scalebox{0.47}{$\rho\hskip-2.04861pt$}}}{{\vphantom{\lim}}{}^{\scalebox{0.35}{$\rho\hskip-2.04861pt$}}}\lim_{n\in\mathchoice{{\vphantom{\mathbb{N}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.6}{$\sigma$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{N}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.56}{$\sigma$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{N}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.47}{$\sigma$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{N}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.35}{$\sigma$}}}\widetilde{\mathbb{N}}\,}}{\mathchoice{{\vphantom{\lim}}{}^{\scalebox{0.6}{$\rho\hskip-2.04861pt$}}}{{\vphantom{\lim}}{}^{\scalebox{0.56}{$\rho\hskip-2.04861pt$}}}{{\vphantom{\lim}}{}^{\scalebox{0.47}{$\rho\hskip-2.04861pt$}}}{{\vphantom{\lim}}{}^{\scalebox{0.35}{$\rho\hskip-2.04861pt$}}}\lim_{n\in\mathchoice{{\vphantom{\mathbb{N}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.6}{$\sigma$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{N}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.56}{$\sigma$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{N}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.47}{$\sigma$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{N}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.35}{$\sigma$}}}\widetilde{\mathbb{N}}\,}}|a_{n}|^{1/n}=\frac{1}{r}

and
r=\text{\rm rad}\left(a_{n}\right)_{\rm c}\in\mathchoice{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.6}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.56}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.47}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.35}{$\rho$}}}\widetilde{\mathbb{R}}
. In Cor.
17
,
we will see the relationship between our definition of radius of convergence
and the least upper bound of all the radii where the HPS converges.

In the following lemma, we show that
\mathchoice{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.6}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.56}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.47}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.35}{$\rho$}}}\widetilde{\mathbb{R}}_{\text{\rm c}}
is a ring:

Lemma 6
.

With pointwise operations,
\mathchoice{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.6}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.56}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.47}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.35}{$\rho$}}}\widetilde{\mathbb{R}}_{\text{\rm c}}
is a
quotient ring.

Proof.

Actually, the result follows from
[
14
, Thm. 3.6]
because
the set

\mathcal{B}:=\{\left(\rho_{\varepsilon}^{-nQ-R}\right)_{n,\varepsilon}\in\mathbb{R}^{\mathbb{N}\times I}\mid Q,\ R\in\mathbb{N}\}

is an asymptotic gauge with respect to the order
(n,\varepsilon)\leq(\bar{n},\bar{\varepsilon})

if and only if
\varepsilon\leq\bar{\varepsilon}
. However, an independent proof
follows the well-known lines of the corresponding proof for the ring

\mathchoice{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.6}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.56}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.47}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.35}{$\rho$}}}\widetilde{\mathbb{R}}
, and depends on the following properties of
\mathcal{B}
:

(a)

\forall p,q\in\mathcal{B}\,\exists r,s\in\mathcal{B}:\ p+q\leq r,\ p\cdot q\leq s
;

(b)

\forall p\in\mathcal{B}\,\exists r,s\in\mathcal{B}:\ r^{-1}+s^{-1}\leq p^{-1}
;

(c)

\forall p,q,r\in\mathcal{B}\,\exists u,v\in\mathcal{B}:\ u^{-1}\cdot q+v^{-1}\cdot r\leq p^{-1}
,

where
p=(p_{n\varepsilon})_{n,\varepsilon}\leq(q_{n\varepsilon})_{n,\varepsilon}=q
means
\forall^{0}\varepsilon\,\forall n\in\mathbb{N}:\ p_{n\varepsilon}\leq q_{n\varepsilon}
.
∎

The following lemma represents a useful tool to deal with the radius
of convergence. It essentially states that the radius of convergence
equals
+\infty
on some subpoint, or it is moderate on some subpoint
or it is greater than any power
\mathchoice{\hbox{\rm d}\rho}{\hbox{\rm d}\rho}{\scalebox{0.75}{$\hbox{\rm d}\rho$}}{\scalebox{0.35}{$\hbox{\rm d}\rho$}}^{-P}
.

Theorem 7
.

Let
\left(a_{n}\right)_{\text{\emph{c}}}\in\mathchoice{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.6}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.56}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.47}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.35}{$\rho$}}}\widetilde{\mathbb{R}}_{\text{\rm c}}

and
r=[r_{\varepsilon}]=\text{\rm rad}\left(a_{n}\right)_{\rm c}\in\mathchoice{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.6}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.56}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.47}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.35}{$\rho$}}}\overline{\mathbb{R}}
, then we have

(i)

r>0
.

(ii)

r<+\infty
or
r=_{\text{\rm s}}+\infty
.

(iii)

If
r<+\infty
, then the following
alternatives hold

(a)

\forall P\in\mathbb{N}:\ r>\mathchoice{\hbox{\rm d}\rho}{\hbox{\rm d}\rho}{\scalebox{0.75}{$\hbox{\rm d}\rho$}}{\scalebox{0.35}{$\hbox{\rm d}\rho$}}^{-P}

or

(b)

setting

\displaystyle\left[r\leq\rho^{-P}\right]:=\left\{\varepsilon\mid r_{\varepsilon}\leq\rho_{\varepsilon}^{-P}\right\}=:L_{P}

\displaystyle P_{\text{m}}:=\min\left\{P\in\mathbb{N}\mid\left[r\leq\rho^{-P}\right]\subseteq_{0}I\right\}

(2.5)

we have

(b.1)

I=\bigcup_{P\in\mathbb{N}}\left[r\leq\rho^{-P}\right]
;

(b.2)

\forall P\geq P_{\text{m}}:\ \left[r\leq\rho^{-P}\right]\subseteq_{0}I,\ r\leq_{L_{P}}\mathchoice{\hbox{\rm d}\rho}{\hbox{\rm d}\rho}{\scalebox{0.75}{$\hbox{\rm d}\rho$}}{\scalebox{0.35}{$\hbox{\rm d}\rho$}}^{-P}
;

(b.3)

\forall P<P_{\text{m}}:\ \mathchoice{\hbox{\rm d}\rho}{\hbox{\rm d}\rho}{\scalebox{0.75}{$\hbox{\rm d}\rho$}}{\scalebox{0.35}{$\hbox{\rm d}\rho$}}^{-P}\leq r
;

(b.4)

If
P_{\text{m}}=0
and
L_{0}^{c}\subseteq_{0}I
,
then
1\leq_{L_{0}^{c}}r
; if
L_{0}^{c}\not\subseteq_{0}I
, then
r\leq 1
.

(iv)

Assume that for all
L\subseteq_{0}I
, the
following implication holds

\left(\exists Q\in\mathbb{N}:\ r\leq_{L}\mathchoice{\hbox{\rm d}\rho}{\hbox{\rm d}\rho}{\scalebox{0.75}{$\hbox{\rm d}\rho$}}{\scalebox{0.35}{$\hbox{\rm d}\rho$}}^{-Q}\right)\text{ or }\left(\forall Q\in\mathbb{N}:\ r>_{L}\mathchoice{\hbox{\rm d}\rho}{\hbox{\rm d}\rho}{\scalebox{0.75}{$\hbox{\rm d}\rho$}}{\scalebox{0.35}{$\hbox{\rm d}\rho$}}^{-Q}\right)\ \Rightarrow\ \forall^{0}\varepsilon\in L:\ \mathcal{P}\left\{r_{\varepsilon}\right\}.

(2.6)

Then
\forall^{0}\varepsilon:\ \mathcal{P}\left\{r_{\varepsilon}\right\}
, i.e. the
property
\mathcal{P}\left\{r_{\varepsilon}\right\}
holds for all sufficiently
small
\varepsilon
.

(v)

If
q\in\mathchoice{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.6}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.56}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.47}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.35}{$\rho$}}}\widetilde{\mathbb{R}}
and
q<r
, then
\exists s\in\mathchoice{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.6}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.56}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.47}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.35}{$\rho$}}}\widetilde{\mathbb{R}}:\ q<s\leq r
.

Proof.

(i)
: Assume that
\left|a_{n\varepsilon}\right|\leq\rho_{\varepsilon}^{-nQ-R}

for all
\varepsilon\leq\varepsilon_{0}
and for all
n\in\mathbb{N}
. Then
\limsup_{n\to+\infty}\left|a_{n\varepsilon}\right|^{1/n}\leq\lim_{n\to+\infty}\rho_{\varepsilon}^{-Q-\frac{R}{n}}=\rho_{\varepsilon}^{-Q}
,
i.e.
r_{\varepsilon}\geq\rho_{\varepsilon}^{Q}
.

(ii)
: Set
L:=\left\{\varepsilon\mid r_{\varepsilon}=+\infty\right\}
.
If
L\subseteq_{0}I
, then
r=_{L}+\infty
. Otherwise
(0,\varepsilon_{0}]\cap L=\emptyset

for some
\varepsilon_{0}
, i.e.
r_{\varepsilon}<+\infty
for all
\varepsilon\leq\varepsilon_{0}
.

(iii)
: Since we assume that
r<+\infty
,
without loss of generality we can take
r_{\varepsilon}<+\infty
for all

\varepsilon
. We also assume that
(iii)(a)
is false,
i.e.
r\leq_{M}\mathchoice{\hbox{\rm d}\rho}{\hbox{\rm d}\rho}{\scalebox{0.75}{$\hbox{\rm d}\rho$}}{\scalebox{0.35}{$\hbox{\rm d}\rho$}}^{-\bar{P}}
for some
\bar{P}\in\mathbb{N}
and
some
M\subseteq_{0}I
. We first prove
(iii)((b))(b.1)
: take

\varepsilon\in\bigcap_{P\in\mathbb{N}}\left[r>\rho^{-P}\right]
, then
r_{\varepsilon}>\rho_{\varepsilon}^{-P}

for all
P\in\mathbb{N}
, so that
r_{\varepsilon}=+\infty
for
P\to+\infty
,
and this is not possible. We also note that
\left[r\leq\rho^{-P}\right]\subseteq\left[r\leq\rho^{-Q}\right]

for all
Q\geq P
. From
M\subseteq_{0}I
and
r\leq_{M}\mathchoice{\hbox{\rm d}\rho}{\hbox{\rm d}\rho}{\scalebox{0.75}{$\hbox{\rm d}\rho$}}{\scalebox{0.35}{$\hbox{\rm d}\rho$}}^{-\bar{P}}
,
we have
(0,\varepsilon_{0}]\cap M\subseteq\left[r\leq\rho^{-(\bar{P}+1)}\right]\subseteq_{0}I
,
and hence definition (
2.5
) yields
P_{\text{m}}\in\mathbb{N}
and
also proves
(iii)((b))(b.2)
. For all
P\in\mathbb{N}_{<P_{\text{m}}}
,
we hence have
\left[r\leq\rho^{-P}\right]\not\subseteq_{0}I
, i.e.
(0,\varepsilon_{P}]\subseteq\left[r>\rho^{-P}\right]

for some
\varepsilon_{P}
. This implies
\mathchoice{\hbox{\rm d}\rho}{\hbox{\rm d}\rho}{\scalebox{0.75}{$\hbox{\rm d}\rho$}}{\scalebox{0.35}{$\hbox{\rm d}\rho$}}^{-P}\leq r
and proves

(iii)((b))(b.3)
. Finally, if
P_{\text{m}}=0
and
L_{P_{\text{m}}}^{c}=L_{0}^{c}\subseteq_{0}I
,
then
1\leq_{L_{0}^{c}}r
because
L_{0}^{c}=\left[r>1\right]
. If

L_{0}^{c}\not\subseteq_{0}I
, then
(0,\varepsilon_{0}]\subseteq L_{0}
for
some
\varepsilon_{0}
, i.e.
r\leq 1
.

(iv)
: By contradiction, assume that
\neg\mathcal{P}\left\{r_{\varepsilon}\right\}

for all
\varepsilon\in L
and for some
L\subseteq_{0}I
. As usual, we assume
that all the results we proved for
\mathchoice{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.6}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.56}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.47}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.35}{$\rho$}}}\overline{\mathbb{R}}
can also be similarly
proved for the restriction
\mathchoice{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.6}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.56}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.47}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.35}{$\rho$}}}\overline{\mathbb{R}}|_{L}
. From
(ii)

for
\mathchoice{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.6}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.56}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.47}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.35}{$\rho$}}}\overline{\mathbb{R}}|_{L}
, we have
r<_{L}+\infty
or
r=_{K}+\infty

for some
K\subseteq_{0}L
. The second case implies
r>_{L}\mathchoice{\hbox{\rm d}\rho}{\hbox{\rm d}\rho}{\scalebox{0.75}{$\hbox{\rm d}\rho$}}{\scalebox{0.35}{$\hbox{\rm d}\rho$}}^{-Q}

for all
Q\in\mathbb{N}
. Since
K\subseteq_{0}I
, we can apply the second alternative
in the implication (
2.6
) to get
\forall^{0}\varepsilon\in K:\ \mathcal{P}\left\{r_{\varepsilon}\right\}
,
which gives a contradiction because
K\subseteq L
. We can hence
consider the first case
r<_{L}+\infty
and apply the subcase
(iii)(a)
,
i.e.
r>_{L}\mathchoice{\hbox{\rm d}\rho}{\hbox{\rm d}\rho}{\scalebox{0.75}{$\hbox{\rm d}\rho$}}{\scalebox{0.35}{$\hbox{\rm d}\rho$}}^{-P}
for all
P\in\mathbb{N}
, and we hence proceed
as above applying the second alternative of the implication (
2.6
).
In the remaining subcase, we can use
(iii)((b))(b.2)
(with
L

instead of
I
). This yields
L_{P_{\text{m}}}\subseteq_{0}L
and
r\leq_{L_{P_{\text{m}}}}\mathchoice{\hbox{\rm d}\rho}{\hbox{\rm d}\rho}{\scalebox{0.75}{$\hbox{\rm d}\rho$}}{\scalebox{0.35}{$\hbox{\rm d}\rho$}}^{-P_{\text{m}}}
.
Since
L_{P_{\text{m}}}\subseteq_{0}I
, we can apply the first alternative
in the implication (
2.6
) to get once again a contradiction.

(v)
: Assume that
r>q
and take
s:=\min(q+1,r)\in\mathchoice{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.6}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.56}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.47}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.35}{$\rho$}}}\widetilde{\mathbb{R}}_{>0}
.
∎

Explicitly note the meaning of Lem.
7
.
(iv)
:
on an arbitrary subpoint
r|_{L}
of the radius of convergence
r=\text{\rm rad}\left(a_{n}\right)_{\rm c}
,
we have to consider only two cases: either
r|_{L}
is
\rho
-moderate
or it is greater than any power
\mathchoice{\hbox{\rm d}\rho}{\hbox{\rm d}\rho}{\scalebox{0.75}{$\hbox{\rm d}\rho$}}{\scalebox{0.35}{$\hbox{\rm d}\rho$}}^{-Q}
(the latter case
including also the case
r|_{L}=+\infty
); if in both cases we are
able to prove the property
\mathcal{P}\left\{r_{\varepsilon}\right\}

for
\varepsilon\in L
sufficiently small, then this property holds for

all

\varepsilon
sufficiently small.

2.3.
Set of convergence

Even if the radius of convergence of the exponential hyperseries is

\text{\rm rad}\left(\frac{1}{n!}\right)_{\rm c}=+\infty
, we have that
e^{x}=\mathchoice{\mathchoice{{\vphantom{\sum}}{}^{\scalebox{0.6}{$\raisebox{-1.59442pt}{$\rho\hskip-5.49028pt$}$}}}{{\vphantom{\sum}}{}^{\scalebox{0.56}{$\raisebox{-1.59442pt}{$\rho\hskip-5.49028pt$}$}}}{{\vphantom{\sum}}{}^{\scalebox{0.47}{$\raisebox{-1.59442pt}{$\rho\hskip-5.49028pt$}$}}}{{\vphantom{\sum}}{}^{\scalebox{0.35}{$\raisebox{-1.59442pt}{$\rho\hskip-5.49028pt$}$}}}\sum_{n\in\mathchoice{{\vphantom{\mathbb{N}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.6}{$\sigma$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{N}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.56}{$\sigma$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{N}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.47}{$\sigma$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{N}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.35}{$\sigma$}}}\widetilde{\mathbb{N}}\,}}{\mathchoice{{\vphantom{\sum}}{}^{\scalebox{0.6}{$\rho\hskip-2.04861pt$}}}{{\vphantom{\sum}}{}^{\scalebox{0.56}{$\rho\hskip-2.04861pt$}}}{{\vphantom{\sum}}{}^{\scalebox{0.47}{$\rho\hskip-2.04861pt$}}}{{\vphantom{\sum}}{}^{\scalebox{0.35}{$\rho\hskip-2.04861pt$}}}\sum_{n\in\mathchoice{{\vphantom{\mathbb{N}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.6}{$\sigma$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{N}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.56}{$\sigma$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{N}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.47}{$\sigma$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{N}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.35}{$\sigma$}}}\widetilde{\mathbb{N}}\,}}{\mathchoice{{\vphantom{\sum}}{}^{\scalebox{0.6}{$\rho\hskip-2.04861pt$}}}{{\vphantom{\sum}}{}^{\scalebox{0.56}{$\rho\hskip-2.04861pt$}}}{{\vphantom{\sum}}{}^{\scalebox{0.47}{$\rho\hskip-2.04861pt$}}}{{\vphantom{\sum}}{}^{\scalebox{0.35}{$\rho\hskip-2.04861pt$}}}\sum_{n\in\mathchoice{{\vphantom{\mathbb{N}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.6}{$\sigma$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{N}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.56}{$\sigma$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{N}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.47}{$\sigma$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{N}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.35}{$\sigma$}}}\widetilde{\mathbb{N}}\,}}{\mathchoice{{\vphantom{\sum}}{}^{\scalebox{0.6}{$\rho\hskip-2.04861pt$}}}{{\vphantom{\sum}}{}^{\scalebox{0.56}{$\rho\hskip-2.04861pt$}}}{{\vphantom{\sum}}{}^{\scalebox{0.47}{$\rho\hskip-2.04861pt$}}}{{\vphantom{\sum}}{}^{\scalebox{0.35}{$\rho\hskip-2.04861pt$}}}\sum_{n\in\mathchoice{{\vphantom{\mathbb{N}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.6}{$\sigma$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{N}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.56}{$\sigma$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{N}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.47}{$\sigma$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{N}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.35}{$\sigma$}}}\widetilde{\mathbb{N}}\,}}\frac{x^{n}}{n!}\in\mathchoice{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.6}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.56}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.47}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.35}{$\rho$}}}\widetilde{\mathbb{R}}

implies
|x|\leq\log\left(\mathchoice{\hbox{\rm d}\rho}{\hbox{\rm d}\rho}{\scalebox{0.75}{$\hbox{\rm d}\rho$}}{\scalebox{0.35}{$\hbox{\rm d}\rho$}}^{-R}\right)
for some
R\in\mathbb{N}
:
in other words, the constraint to get a
\rho
-moderate number implies
that even if
\mathchoice{\mathchoice{{\vphantom{\sum}}{}^{\scalebox{0.6}{$\raisebox{-1.59442pt}{$\rho\hskip-5.49028pt$}$}}}{{\vphantom{\sum}}{}^{\scalebox{0.56}{$\raisebox{-1.59442pt}{$\rho\hskip-5.49028pt$}$}}}{{\vphantom{\sum}}{}^{\scalebox{0.47}{$\raisebox{-1.59442pt}{$\rho\hskip-5.49028pt$}$}}}{{\vphantom{\sum}}{}^{\scalebox{0.35}{$\raisebox{-1.59442pt}{$\rho\hskip-5.49028pt$}$}}}\sum_{n\in\mathchoice{{\vphantom{\mathbb{N}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.6}{$\sigma$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{N}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.56}{$\sigma$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{N}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.47}{$\sigma$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{N}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.35}{$\sigma$}}}\widetilde{\mathbb{N}}\,}}{\mathchoice{{\vphantom{\sum}}{}^{\scalebox{0.6}{$\rho\hskip-2.04861pt$}}}{{\vphantom{\sum}}{}^{\scalebox{0.56}{$\rho\hskip-2.04861pt$}}}{{\vphantom{\sum}}{}^{\scalebox{0.47}{$\rho\hskip-2.04861pt$}}}{{\vphantom{\sum}}{}^{\scalebox{0.35}{$\rho\hskip-2.04861pt$}}}\sum_{n\in\mathchoice{{\vphantom{\mathbb{N}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.6}{$\sigma$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{N}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.56}{$\sigma$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{N}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.47}{$\sigma$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{N}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.35}{$\sigma$}}}\widetilde{\mathbb{N}}\,}}{\mathchoice{{\vphantom{\sum}}{}^{\scalebox{0.6}{$\rho\hskip-2.04861pt$}}}{{\vphantom{\sum}}{}^{\scalebox{0.56}{$\rho\hskip-2.04861pt$}}}{{\vphantom{\sum}}{}^{\scalebox{0.47}{$\rho\hskip-2.04861pt$}}}{{\vphantom{\sum}}{}^{\scalebox{0.35}{$\rho\hskip-2.04861pt$}}}\sum_{n\in\mathchoice{{\vphantom{\mathbb{N}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.6}{$\sigma$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{N}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.56}{$\sigma$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{N}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.47}{$\sigma$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{N}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.35}{$\sigma$}}}\widetilde{\mathbb{N}}\,}}{\mathchoice{{\vphantom{\sum}}{}^{\scalebox{0.6}{$\rho\hskip-2.04861pt$}}}{{\vphantom{\sum}}{}^{\scalebox{0.56}{$\rho\hskip-2.04861pt$}}}{{\vphantom{\sum}}{}^{\scalebox{0.47}{$\rho\hskip-2.04861pt$}}}{{\vphantom{\sum}}{}^{\scalebox{0.35}{$\rho\hskip-2.04861pt$}}}\sum_{n\in\mathchoice{{\vphantom{\mathbb{N}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.6}{$\sigma$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{N}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.56}{$\sigma$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{N}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.47}{$\sigma$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{N}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.35}{$\sigma$}}}\widetilde{\mathbb{N}}\,}}\frac{x^{n}}{n!}
converges
at
x
, the exponential HPS does
not
converge in the interval

[x,\text{\rm rad}\left(\frac{1}{n!}\right)_{\rm c})=[x,+\infty)\subseteq\mathchoice{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.6}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.56}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.47}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.35}{$\rho$}}}\widetilde{\mathbb{R}}
.

Moreover, in all our examples, if the HPS
\mathchoice{\mathchoice{{\vphantom{\sum}}{}^{\scalebox{0.6}{$\raisebox{-1.59442pt}{$\rho\hskip-5.49028pt$}$}}}{{\vphantom{\sum}}{}^{\scalebox{0.56}{$\raisebox{-1.59442pt}{$\rho\hskip-5.49028pt$}$}}}{{\vphantom{\sum}}{}^{\scalebox{0.47}{$\raisebox{-1.59442pt}{$\rho\hskip-5.49028pt$}$}}}{{\vphantom{\sum}}{}^{\scalebox{0.35}{$\raisebox{-1.59442pt}{$\rho\hskip-5.49028pt$}$}}}\sum_{n\in\mathchoice{{\vphantom{\mathbb{N}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.6}{$\sigma$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{N}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.56}{$\sigma$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{N}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.47}{$\sigma$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{N}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.35}{$\sigma$}}}\widetilde{\mathbb{N}}\,}}{\mathchoice{{\vphantom{\sum}}{}^{\scalebox{0.6}{$\rho\hskip-2.04861pt$}}}{{\vphantom{\sum}}{}^{\scalebox{0.56}{$\rho\hskip-2.04861pt$}}}{{\vphantom{\sum}}{}^{\scalebox{0.47}{$\rho\hskip-2.04861pt$}}}{{\vphantom{\sum}}{}^{\scalebox{0.35}{$\rho\hskip-2.04861pt$}}}\sum_{n\in\mathchoice{{\vphantom{\mathbb{N}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.6}{$\sigma$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{N}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.56}{$\sigma$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{N}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.47}{$\sigma$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{N}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.35}{$\sigma$}}}\widetilde{\mathbb{N}}\,}}{\mathchoice{{\vphantom{\sum}}{}^{\scalebox{0.6}{$\rho\hskip-2.04861pt$}}}{{\vphantom{\sum}}{}^{\scalebox{0.56}{$\rho\hskip-2.04861pt$}}}{{\vphantom{\sum}}{}^{\scalebox{0.47}{$\rho\hskip-2.04861pt$}}}{{\vphantom{\sum}}{}^{\scalebox{0.35}{$\rho\hskip-2.04861pt$}}}\sum_{n\in\mathchoice{{\vphantom{\mathbb{N}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.6}{$\sigma$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{N}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.56}{$\sigma$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{N}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.47}{$\sigma$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{N}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.35}{$\sigma$}}}\widetilde{\mathbb{N}}\,}}{\mathchoice{{\vphantom{\sum}}{}^{\scalebox{0.6}{$\rho\hskip-2.04861pt$}}}{{\vphantom{\sum}}{}^{\scalebox{0.56}{$\rho\hskip-2.04861pt$}}}{{\vphantom{\sum}}{}^{\scalebox{0.47}{$\rho\hskip-2.04861pt$}}}{{\vphantom{\sum}}{}^{\scalebox{0.35}{$\rho\hskip-2.04861pt$}}}\sum_{n\in\mathchoice{{\vphantom{\mathbb{N}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.6}{$\sigma$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{N}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.56}{$\sigma$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{N}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.47}{$\sigma$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{N}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.35}{$\sigma$}}}\widetilde{\mathbb{N}}\,}}a_{n}(x-c)^{n}\in\mathchoice{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.6}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.56}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.47}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.35}{$\rho$}}}\widetilde{\mathbb{R}}

converges, then it converges exactly to
\left[\sum_{n=0}^{+\infty}a_{n\varepsilon}(x_{\varepsilon}-c_{\varepsilon})^{n}\right]\in\mathchoice{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.6}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.56}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.47}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.35}{$\rho$}}}\widetilde{\mathbb{R}}
.
The following definition of
set of convergence
closely recalls
the definition of GSF:

Definition 8
.

Let
\left(a_{n}\right)_{\text{{\rm c}}}\in\mathchoice{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.6}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.56}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.47}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.35}{$\rho$}}}\widetilde{\mathbb{R}}_{\text{\rm c}}

and
c\in\mathchoice{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.6}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.56}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.47}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.35}{$\rho$}}}\widetilde{\mathbb{R}}
. The set of convergence

\mathchoice{{\vphantom{\text{\rm c}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.6}{$\rho$}}_{\scalebox{0.6}{$\sigma$}}}{{\vphantom{\text{\rm c}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.56}{$\rho$}}_{\scalebox{0.56}{$\sigma$}}}{{\vphantom{\text{\rm c}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.47}{$\rho$}}_{\scalebox{0.47}{$\sigma$}}}{{\vphantom{\text{\rm c}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.35}{$\rho$}}_{\scalebox{0.35}{$\sigma$}}}\text{\rm conv}\left(\left(a_{n}\right)_{\text{\rm c}},c\right)

is the set of all
x\in\mathchoice{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.6}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.56}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.47}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.35}{$\rho$}}}\widetilde{\mathbb{R}}
satisfying

(i)

\left|x-c\right|<\text{\rm rad}\left(a_{n}\right)_{\rm c}
,

and such that there exist representatives
[x_{\varepsilon}]=x
,
\left[a_{n\varepsilon}\right]_{\text{{\rm c}}}=\left(a_{n}\right)_{\text{{\rm c}}}

and
[c_{\varepsilon}]=c
satisfying the following conditions:

(ii)

\left[a_{n\varepsilon}\cdot\left(x_{\varepsilon}-c_{\varepsilon}\right)^{n}\right]_{\text{{\rm s}}}\in\mathchoice{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.6}{$\rho$}}_{\scalebox{0.6}{$\sigma$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.56}{$\rho$}}_{\scalebox{0.56}{$\sigma$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.47}{$\rho$}}_{\scalebox{0.47}{$\sigma$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.35}{$\rho$}}_{\scalebox{0.35}{$\sigma$}}}\widetilde{\mathbb{R}}\llbracket x-c\rrbracket
,
i.e. we have a formal HPS;

(iii)

\mathchoice{\mathchoice{{\vphantom{\sum}}{}^{\scalebox{0.6}{$\raisebox{-1.59442pt}{$\rho\hskip-5.49028pt$}$}}}{{\vphantom{\sum}}{}^{\scalebox{0.56}{$\raisebox{-1.59442pt}{$\rho\hskip-5.49028pt$}$}}}{{\vphantom{\sum}}{}^{\scalebox{0.47}{$\raisebox{-1.59442pt}{$\rho\hskip-5.49028pt$}$}}}{{\vphantom{\sum}}{}^{\scalebox{0.35}{$\raisebox{-1.59442pt}{$\rho\hskip-5.49028pt$}$}}}\sum_{n\in\mathchoice{{\vphantom{\mathbb{N}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.6}{$\sigma$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{N}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.56}{$\sigma$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{N}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.47}{$\sigma$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{N}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.35}{$\sigma$}}}\widetilde{\mathbb{N}}\,}}{\mathchoice{{\vphantom{\sum}}{}^{\scalebox{0.6}{$\rho\hskip-2.04861pt$}}}{{\vphantom{\sum}}{}^{\scalebox{0.56}{$\rho\hskip-2.04861pt$}}}{{\vphantom{\sum}}{}^{\scalebox{0.47}{$\rho\hskip-2.04861pt$}}}{{\vphantom{\sum}}{}^{\scalebox{0.35}{$\rho\hskip-2.04861pt$}}}\sum_{n\in\mathchoice{{\vphantom{\mathbb{N}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.6}{$\sigma$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{N}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.56}{$\sigma$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{N}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.47}{$\sigma$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{N}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.35}{$\sigma$}}}\widetilde{\mathbb{N}}\,}}{\mathchoice{{\vphantom{\sum}}{}^{\scalebox{0.6}{$\rho\hskip-2.04861pt$}}}{{\vphantom{\sum}}{}^{\scalebox{0.56}{$\rho\hskip-2.04861pt$}}}{{\vphantom{\sum}}{}^{\scalebox{0.47}{$\rho\hskip-2.04861pt$}}}{{\vphantom{\sum}}{}^{\scalebox{0.35}{$\rho\hskip-2.04861pt$}}}\sum_{n\in\mathchoice{{\vphantom{\mathbb{N}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.6}{$\sigma$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{N}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.56}{$\sigma$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{N}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.47}{$\sigma$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{N}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.35}{$\sigma$}}}\widetilde{\mathbb{N}}\,}}{\mathchoice{{\vphantom{\sum}}{}^{\scalebox{0.6}{$\rho\hskip-2.04861pt$}}}{{\vphantom{\sum}}{}^{\scalebox{0.56}{$\rho\hskip-2.04861pt$}}}{{\vphantom{\sum}}{}^{\scalebox{0.47}{$\rho\hskip-2.04861pt$}}}{{\vphantom{\sum}}{}^{\scalebox{0.35}{$\rho\hskip-2.04861pt$}}}\sum_{n\in\mathchoice{{\vphantom{\mathbb{N}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.6}{$\sigma$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{N}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.56}{$\sigma$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{N}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.47}{$\sigma$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{N}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.35}{$\sigma$}}}\widetilde{\mathbb{N}}\,}}a_{n}(x-c)^{n}=\left[\sum_{n=0}^{+\infty}a_{n\varepsilon}(x_{\varepsilon}-c_{\varepsilon})^{n}\right]\in\mathchoice{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.6}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.56}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.47}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.35}{$\rho$}}}\widetilde{\mathbb{R}}
;

(iv)

For all representatives
[\bar{x}_{\varepsilon}]=x

and all
k\in\mathbb{N}_{>0}
, the
k
-th derivative net is
\rho
-moderate:

\left(\frac{\mathchoice{\hbox{\rm d}}{\hbox{\rm d}}{\scalebox{0.75}{$\hbox{\rm d}$}}{\scalebox{0.35}{$\hbox{\rm d}$}}^{k}}{\mathchoice{\hbox{\rm d}x}{\hbox{\rm d}x}{\scalebox{0.75}{$\hbox{\rm d}x$}}{\scalebox{0.35}{$\hbox{\rm d}x$}}^{k}}\left(\sum_{n=0}^{+\infty}a_{n\varepsilon}(x-c_{\varepsilon})^{n}\right)_{x=\bar{x}_{\varepsilon}}\right)\in\mathbb{R}_{\rho}.

Note that condition
(ii)
is necessary because
in
(iii)
we use a HPS; on the other hand, conditions

(iii)
and
(iv)
state that the function

x\in\mathchoice{{\vphantom{\text{\rm c}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.6}{$\rho$}}_{\scalebox{0.6}{$\sigma$}}}{{\vphantom{\text{\rm c}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.56}{$\rho$}}_{\scalebox{0.56}{$\sigma$}}}{{\vphantom{\text{\rm c}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.47}{$\rho$}}_{\scalebox{0.47}{$\sigma$}}}{{\vphantom{\text{\rm c}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.35}{$\rho$}}_{\scalebox{0.35}{$\sigma$}}}\text{\rm conv}\left(\left(a_{n}\right)_{\text{\rm c}},c\right)\mapsto\mathchoice{\mathchoice{{\vphantom{\sum}}{}^{\scalebox{0.6}{$\raisebox{-1.59442pt}{$\rho\hskip-5.49028pt$}$}}}{{\vphantom{\sum}}{}^{\scalebox{0.56}{$\raisebox{-1.59442pt}{$\rho\hskip-5.49028pt$}$}}}{{\vphantom{\sum}}{}^{\scalebox{0.47}{$\raisebox{-1.59442pt}{$\rho\hskip-5.49028pt$}$}}}{{\vphantom{\sum}}{}^{\scalebox{0.35}{$\raisebox{-1.59442pt}{$\rho\hskip-5.49028pt$}$}}}\sum_{n\in\mathchoice{{\vphantom{\mathbb{N}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.6}{$\sigma$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{N}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.56}{$\sigma$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{N}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.47}{$\sigma$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{N}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.35}{$\sigma$}}}\widetilde{\mathbb{N}}\,}}{\mathchoice{{\vphantom{\sum}}{}^{\scalebox{0.6}{$\rho\hskip-2.04861pt$}}}{{\vphantom{\sum}}{}^{\scalebox{0.56}{$\rho\hskip-2.04861pt$}}}{{\vphantom{\sum}}{}^{\scalebox{0.47}{$\rho\hskip-2.04861pt$}}}{{\vphantom{\sum}}{}^{\scalebox{0.35}{$\rho\hskip-2.04861pt$}}}\sum_{n\in\mathchoice{{\vphantom{\mathbb{N}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.6}{$\sigma$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{N}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.56}{$\sigma$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{N}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.47}{$\sigma$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{N}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.35}{$\sigma$}}}\widetilde{\mathbb{N}}\,}}{\mathchoice{{\vphantom{\sum}}{}^{\scalebox{0.6}{$\rho\hskip-2.04861pt$}}}{{\vphantom{\sum}}{}^{\scalebox{0.56}{$\rho\hskip-2.04861pt$}}}{{\vphantom{\sum}}{}^{\scalebox{0.47}{$\rho\hskip-2.04861pt$}}}{{\vphantom{\sum}}{}^{\scalebox{0.35}{$\rho\hskip-2.04861pt$}}}\sum_{n\in\mathchoice{{\vphantom{\mathbb{N}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.6}{$\sigma$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{N}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.56}{$\sigma$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{N}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.47}{$\sigma$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{N}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.35}{$\sigma$}}}\widetilde{\mathbb{N}}\,}}{\mathchoice{{\vphantom{\sum}}{}^{\scalebox{0.6}{$\rho\hskip-2.04861pt$}}}{{\vphantom{\sum}}{}^{\scalebox{0.56}{$\rho\hskip-2.04861pt$}}}{{\vphantom{\sum}}{}^{\scalebox{0.47}{$\rho\hskip-2.04861pt$}}}{{\vphantom{\sum}}{}^{\scalebox{0.35}{$\rho\hskip-2.04861pt$}}}\sum_{n\in\mathchoice{{\vphantom{\mathbb{N}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.6}{$\sigma$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{N}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.56}{$\sigma$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{N}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.47}{$\sigma$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{N}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.35}{$\sigma$}}}\widetilde{\mathbb{N}}\,}}a_{n}(x-c)^{n}\in\mathchoice{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.6}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.56}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.47}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.35}{$\rho$}}}\widetilde{\mathbb{R}}

is a GSF defined by the net of smooth functions
\left(\sum_{n=0}^{+\infty}a_{n\varepsilon}(x_{\varepsilon}-c_{\varepsilon})^{n}\right)
.
As for GSF, see
[
13
, Thm. 16]
, condition
(iv)

will be useful to prove that we have independence from representatives
of
x
in all the derivatives. In Cor.
25
,
we will see that under very general assumptions and if
\sigma\leq\rho^{*}
,
condition
(iv)
can be omitted.

In Sec.
14
we will show that
\log\left(\mathchoice{\hbox{\rm d}\rho}{\hbox{\rm d}\rho}{\scalebox{0.75}{$\hbox{\rm d}\rho$}}{\scalebox{0.35}{$\hbox{\rm d}\rho$}}^{-1}\right)\in\mathchoice{{\vphantom{\text{\rm c}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.6}{$\rho$}}_{\scalebox{0.6}{$\sigma$}}}{{\vphantom{\text{\rm c}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.56}{$\rho$}}_{\scalebox{0.56}{$\sigma$}}}{{\vphantom{\text{\rm c}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.47}{$\rho$}}_{\scalebox{0.47}{$\sigma$}}}{{\vphantom{\text{\rm c}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.35}{$\rho$}}_{\scalebox{0.35}{$\sigma$}}}\text{\rm conv}\left(\left(\frac{1}{n!}\right)_{\text{\rm c}},0\right)

(the set of convergence of the exponential HPS at the origin), but

\mathchoice{\hbox{\rm d}\rho}{\hbox{\rm d}\rho}{\scalebox{0.75}{$\hbox{\rm d}\rho$}}{\scalebox{0.35}{$\hbox{\rm d}\rho$}}^{-1}\notin\text{conv}\left(\left(\frac{1}{n!}\right)_{n}^{\text{{\rm c}}},0\right)
.
We immediately note that
x\in\text{conv}\left(\left(a_{n}\right)_{n}^{\text{c}},c\right)

if and only if
x-c\in\text{conv}\left(\left(a_{n}\right)_{n}^{\text{c}},0\right)
,
and because of this property without loss of generality we will frequently
assume
c=0
.

We also note that condition
(iii)
states that the hyperseries

\mathchoice{\mathchoice{{\vphantom{\sum}}{}^{\scalebox{0.6}{$\raisebox{-1.59442pt}{$\rho\hskip-5.49028pt$}$}}}{{\vphantom{\sum}}{}^{\scalebox{0.56}{$\raisebox{-1.59442pt}{$\rho\hskip-5.49028pt$}$}}}{{\vphantom{\sum}}{}^{\scalebox{0.47}{$\raisebox{-1.59442pt}{$\rho\hskip-5.49028pt$}$}}}{{\vphantom{\sum}}{}^{\scalebox{0.35}{$\raisebox{-1.59442pt}{$\rho\hskip-5.49028pt$}$}}}\sum_{n\in\mathchoice{{\vphantom{\mathbb{N}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.6}{$\sigma$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{N}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.56}{$\sigma$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{N}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.47}{$\sigma$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{N}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.35}{$\sigma$}}}\widetilde{\mathbb{N}}\,}}{\mathchoice{{\vphantom{\sum}}{}^{\scalebox{0.6}{$\rho\hskip-2.04861pt$}}}{{\vphantom{\sum}}{}^{\scalebox{0.56}{$\rho\hskip-2.04861pt$}}}{{\vphantom{\sum}}{}^{\scalebox{0.47}{$\rho\hskip-2.04861pt$}}}{{\vphantom{\sum}}{}^{\scalebox{0.35}{$\rho\hskip-2.04861pt$}}}\sum_{n\in\mathchoice{{\vphantom{\mathbb{N}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.6}{$\sigma$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{N}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.56}{$\sigma$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{N}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.47}{$\sigma$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{N}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.35}{$\sigma$}}}\widetilde{\mathbb{N}}\,}}{\mathchoice{{\vphantom{\sum}}{}^{\scalebox{0.6}{$\rho\hskip-2.04861pt$}}}{{\vphantom{\sum}}{}^{\scalebox{0.56}{$\rho\hskip-2.04861pt$}}}{{\vphantom{\sum}}{}^{\scalebox{0.47}{$\rho\hskip-2.04861pt$}}}{{\vphantom{\sum}}{}^{\scalebox{0.35}{$\rho\hskip-2.04861pt$}}}\sum_{n\in\mathchoice{{\vphantom{\mathbb{N}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.6}{$\sigma$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{N}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.56}{$\sigma$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{N}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.47}{$\sigma$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{N}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.35}{$\sigma$}}}\widetilde{\mathbb{N}}\,}}{\mathchoice{{\vphantom{\sum}}{}^{\scalebox{0.6}{$\rho\hskip-2.04861pt$}}}{{\vphantom{\sum}}{}^{\scalebox{0.56}{$\rho\hskip-2.04861pt$}}}{{\vphantom{\sum}}{}^{\scalebox{0.47}{$\rho\hskip-2.04861pt$}}}{{\vphantom{\sum}}{}^{\scalebox{0.35}{$\rho\hskip-2.04861pt$}}}\sum_{n\in\mathchoice{{\vphantom{\mathb
