n}\,}}{\mathchoice{{\vphantom{\sum}}{}^{\scalebox{0.6}{$\rho\,\,\,\hskip-2.04861pt$}}}{{\vphantom{\sum}}{}^{\scalebox{0.56}{$\rho\,\,\,\hskip-2.04861pt$}}}{{\vphantom{\sum}}{}^{\scalebox{0.47}{$\rho\,\,\,\hskip-2.04861pt$}}}{{\vphantom{\sum}}{}^{\scalebox{0.35}{$\rho\,\,\,\hskip-2.04861pt$}}}\sum_{m\in\mathchoice{{\vphantom{\mathbb{N}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.6}{$\sigma$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{N}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.56}{$\sigma$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{N}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.47}{$\sigma$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{N}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.35}{$\sigma$}}}\widetilde{\mathbb{N}}_{\geq n}\,}}{\mathchoice{{\vphantom{\sum}}{}^{\scalebox{0.6}{$\rho\,\,\,\hskip-2.04861pt$}}}{{\vphantom{\sum}}{}^{\scalebox{0.56}{$\rho\,\,\,\hskip-2.04861pt$}}}{{\vphantom{\sum}}{}^{\scalebox{0.47}{$\rho\,\,\,\hskip-2.04861pt$}}}{{\vphantom{\sum}}{}^{\scalebox{0.35}{$\rho\,\,\,\hskip-2.04861pt$}}}\sum_{m\in\mathchoice{{\vphantom{\mathbb{N}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.6}{$\sigma$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{N}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.56}{$\sigma$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{N}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.47}{$\sigma$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{N}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.35}{$\sigma$}}}\widetilde{\mathbb{N}}_{\geq n}\,}}\frac{f^{(m)}(c)}{m!}n!{m\choose n}(x-c)^{m-n}

\displaystyle=\left[\sum_{n=0}^{+\infty}\frac{(x_{\varepsilon}-\bar{c}_{\varepsilon})^{n}}{n!}\sum_{m\geq n}^{+\infty}\frac{f_{\varepsilon}^{(m)}(c_{\varepsilon})}{m!}n!{m\choose n}(x_{\varepsilon}-c_{\varepsilon})^{m-n}\right].

Therefore, the usual proof, see e.g.
[
22
]
, yields

\sum_{n=0}^{+\infty}\frac{(x_{\varepsilon}-\bar{c}_{\varepsilon})^{n}}{n!}\sum_{m\geq n}^{+\infty}\frac{f_{\varepsilon}^{(m)}(c_{\varepsilon})}{m!}n!{m\choose n}(x_{\varepsilon}-c_{\varepsilon})^{m-n}=\sum_{n=0}^{+\infty}\frac{f_{\varepsilon}^{(n)}(c)}{n!}(x_{\varepsilon}-c_{\varepsilon})^{n}

and hence
f(x)=\mathchoice{\mathchoice{{\vphantom{\sum}}{}^{\scalebox{0.6}{$\raisebox{-1.59442pt}{$\rho\hskip-5.49028pt$}$}}}{{\vphantom{\sum}}{}^{\scalebox{0.56}{$\raisebox{-1.59442pt}{$\rho\hskip-5.49028pt$}$}}}{{\vphantom{\sum}}{}^{\scalebox{0.47}{$\raisebox{-1.59442pt}{$\rho\hskip-5.49028pt$}$}}}{{\vphantom{\sum}}{}^{\scalebox{0.35}{$\raisebox{-1.59442pt}{$\rho\hskip-5.49028pt$}$}}}\sum_{n\in\mathchoice{{\vphantom{\mathbb{N}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.6}{$\sigma$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{N}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.56}{$\sigma$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{N}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.47}{$\sigma$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{N}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.35}{$\sigma$}}}\widetilde{\mathbb{N}}\,}}{\mathchoice{{\vphantom{\sum}}{}^{\scalebox{0.6}{$\rho\hskip-2.04861pt$}}}{{\vphantom{\sum}}{}^{\scalebox{0.56}{$\rho\hskip-2.04861pt$}}}{{\vphantom{\sum}}{}^{\scalebox{0.47}{$\rho\hskip-2.04861pt$}}}{{\vphantom{\sum}}{}^{\scalebox{0.35}{$\rho\hskip-2.04861pt$}}}\sum_{n\in\mathchoice{{\vphantom{\mathbb{N}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.6}{$\sigma$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{N}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.56}{$\sigma$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{N}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.47}{$\sigma$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{N}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.35}{$\sigma$}}}\widetilde{\mathbb{N}}\,}}{\mathchoice{{\vphantom{\sum}}{}^{\scalebox{0.6}{$\rho\hskip-2.04861pt$}}}{{\vphantom{\sum}}{}^{\scalebox{0.56}{$\rho\hskip-2.04861pt$}}}{{\vphantom{\sum}}{}^{\scalebox{0.47}{$\rho\hskip-2.04861pt$}}}{{\vphantom{\sum}}{}^{\scalebox{0.35}{$\rho\hskip-2.04861pt$}}}\sum_{n\in\mathchoice{{\vphantom{\mathbb{N}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.6}{$\sigma$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{N}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.56}{$\sigma$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{N}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.47}{$\sigma$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{N}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.35}{$\sigma$}}}\widetilde{\mathbb{N}}\,}}{\mathchoice{{\vphantom{\sum}}{}^{\scalebox{0.6}{$\rho\hskip-2.04861pt$}}}{{\vphantom{\sum}}{}^{\scalebox{0.56}{$\rho\hskip-2.04861pt$}}}{{\vphantom{\sum}}{}^{\scalebox{0.47}{$\rho\hskip-2.04861pt$}}}{{\vphantom{\sum}}{}^{\scalebox{0.35}{$\rho\hskip-2.04861pt$}}}\sum_{n\in\mathchoice{{\vphantom{\mathbb{N}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.6}{$\sigma$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{N}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.56}{$\sigma$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{N}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.47}{$\sigma$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{N}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.35}{$\sigma$}}}\widetilde{\mathbb{N}}\,}}\frac{f^{(n)}(c)}{n!}(x-c)^{n}=\left[\sum_{n=0}^{+\infty}\frac{f_{\varepsilon}^{(n)}(c)}{n!}(x_{\varepsilon}-c_{\varepsilon})^{n}\right]
,
which implies the conclusion.
∎

4.
Characterization of generalized real analytic functions, inversion
and identity principle

The classical characterization of real analytic functions by the growth
rate of the derivatives establishes a difference between GRAF and
Colombeau real analytic functions:

Theorem 37
.

Let
\sigma\leq\rho^{*}
,
U
be a sharply
open set of
\mathchoice{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.6}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.56}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.47}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.35}{$\rho$}}}\widetilde{\mathbb{R}}
, and
f\in\mathchoice{{\vphantom{\mathcal{G}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.6}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathcal{G}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.56}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathcal{G}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.47}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathcal{G}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.35}{$\rho$}}}\mathcal{GC}^{\infty}(U,\mathchoice{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.6}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.56}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.47}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.35}{$\rho$}}}\widetilde{\mathbb{R}})
be a GSF defined by
the net
(f_{\varepsilon})
. Then
f\in\mathchoice{{\vphantom{\mathcal{G}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.6}{$\rho$}}_{\scalebox{0.6}{$\sigma$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathcal{G}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.56}{$\rho$}}_{\scalebox{0.56}{$\sigma$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathcal{G}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.47}{$\rho$}}_{\scalebox{0.47}{$\sigma$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathcal{G}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.35}{$\rho$}}_{\scalebox{0.35}{$\sigma$}}}\mathcal{GC}^{\omega}(U,\mathchoice{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.6}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.56}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.47}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.35}{$\rho$}}}\widetilde{\mathbb{R}})
if and only if for
each
c\in U
there exist
s=[s_{\varepsilon}]
,
C=\left[C_{\varepsilon}\right]
,

R=\left[R_{\varepsilon}\right]\in\mathchoice{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.6}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.56}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.47}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.35}{$\rho$}}}\widetilde{\mathbb{R}}_{>0}
such that
B_{s}(c)\subseteq U

and

\forall[x_{\varepsilon}]\in B_{s}(c)\,\forall^{0}\varepsilon\,\forall n\in\mathbb{N}:\ \left|f_{\varepsilon}^{(n)}(x_{\varepsilon})\right|\leq C_{\varepsilon}\frac{n!}{R_{\varepsilon}^{n}}.

(4.1)

Proof.

We prove that condition (
4.1
) is necessary. For
c\in U
,
we have
f(x)=\mathchoice{\mathchoice{{\vphantom{\sum}}{}^{\scalebox{0.6}{$\raisebox{-1.59442pt}{$\rho\hskip-5.49028pt$}$}}}{{\vphantom{\sum}}{}^{\scalebox{0.56}{$\raisebox{-1.59442pt}{$\rho\hskip-5.49028pt$}$}}}{{\vphantom{\sum}}{}^{\scalebox{0.47}{$\raisebox{-1.59442pt}{$\rho\hskip-5.49028pt$}$}}}{{\vphantom{\sum}}{}^{\scalebox{0.35}{$\raisebox{-1.59442pt}{$\rho\hskip-5.49028pt$}$}}}\sum_{n\in\mathchoice{{\vphantom{\mathbb{N}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.6}{$\sigma$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{N}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.56}{$\sigma$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{N}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.47}{$\sigma$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{N}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.35}{$\sigma$}}}\widetilde{\mathbb{N}}\,}}{\mathchoice{{\vphantom{\sum}}{}^{\scalebox{0.6}{$\rho\hskip-2.04861pt$}}}{{\vphantom{\sum}}{}^{\scalebox{0.56}{$\rho\hskip-2.04861pt$}}}{{\vphantom{\sum}}{}^{\scalebox{0.47}{$\rho\hskip-2.04861pt$}}}{{\vphantom{\sum}}{}^{\scalebox{0.35}{$\rho\hskip-2.04861pt$}}}\sum_{n\in\mathchoice{{\vphantom{\mathbb{N}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.6}{$\sigma$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{N}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.56}{$\sigma$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{N}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.47}{$\sigma$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{N}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.35}{$\sigma$}}}\widetilde{\mathbb{N}}\,}}{\mathchoice{{\vphantom{\sum}}{}^{\scalebox{0.6}{$\rho\hskip-2.04861pt$}}}{{\vphantom{\sum}}{}^{\scalebox{0.56}{$\rho\hskip-2.04861pt$}}}{{\vphantom{\sum}}{}^{\scalebox{0.47}{$\rho\hskip-2.04861pt$}}}{{\vphantom{\sum}}{}^{\scalebox{0.35}{$\rho\hskip-2.04861pt$}}}\sum_{n\in\mathchoice{{\vphantom{\mathbb{N}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.6}{$\sigma$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{N}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.56}{$\sigma$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{N}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.47}{$\sigma$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{N}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.35}{$\sigma$}}}\widetilde{\mathbb{N}}\,}}{\mathchoice{{\vphantom{\sum}}{}^{\scalebox{0.6}{$\rho\hskip-2.04861pt$}}}{{\vphantom{\sum}}{}^{\scalebox{0.56}{$\rho\hskip-2.04861pt$}}}{{\vphantom{\sum}}{}^{\scalebox{0.47}{$\rho\hskip-2.04861pt$}}}{{\vphantom{\sum}}{}^{\scalebox{0.35}{$\rho\hskip-2.04861pt$}}}\sum_{n\in\mathchoice{{\vphantom{\mathbb{N}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.6}{$\sigma$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{N}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.56}{$\sigma$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{N}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.47}{$\sigma$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{N}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.35}{$\sigma$}}}\widetilde{\mathbb{N}}\,}}\frac{f^{(n)}(c)}{n!}\left(x-c\right){}^{n}

for all
x\in(c-\bar{s},c+\bar{s})
for some
\bar{s}>0
from Def.
30

and Thm.
33
. We first note that condition (
4.1
)
can also be formulated as an inequality in
\mathchoice{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.6}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.56}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.47}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.35}{$\rho$}}}\widetilde{\mathbb{R}}_{\text{\rm c}}
and as such
it does not depend on the representatives involved. Therefore, from
Thm.
28
and Thm.
33
, without
loss of generality, we can assume that the given net
(f_{\varepsilon})

is of real analytic functions satisfying
f_{\varepsilon}(x)=\sum_{n=0}^{+\infty}\frac{f_{\varepsilon}^{(n)}(c_{\varepsilon})}{n!}(x-c_{\varepsilon})^{n}

for all
x\in(c_{\varepsilon}-\text{\rm rad}\left(a_{n}\right)_{{\rm c}{\varepsilon}},c_{\varepsilon}+\text{\rm rad}\left(a_{n}\right)_{{\rm c}{\varepsilon}})
.
From Lem.
21
, locally the Taylor summands

\left(\frac{f^{(n)}(c)}{n!}(\bar{x}-c)^{n}\right)_{n\in\mathbb{N}}
are
eventually
\mathchoice{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.6}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.56}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.47}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.35}{$\rho$}}}\widetilde{\mathbb{R}}
-bounded in
\mathchoice{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.6}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.56}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.47}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.35}{$\rho$}}}\widetilde{\mathbb{R}}_{\text{\rm c}}
if
\bar{x}
is sufficiently
near to
c=[c_{\varepsilon}]
, i.e. there exists
\sigma\in\mathchoice{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.6}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.56}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.47}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.35}{$\rho$}}}\widetilde{\mathbb{R}}_{>0}

such that for each
\bar{x}=[\bar{x}_{\varepsilon}]\in B_{\sigma}(c)
we
have

\forall^{0}\varepsilon\,\forall j\in\mathbb{N}:\ \left|\frac{f_{\varepsilon}^{(j)}(c_{\varepsilon})}{j!}(\bar{x}_{\varepsilon}-c_{\varepsilon})^{j}\right|\leq K_{\varepsilon},

(4.2)

for some
K=[K_{\varepsilon}]\in\mathchoice{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.6}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.56}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.47}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.35}{$\rho$}}}\widetilde{\mathbb{R}}
. Set
s:=\frac{1}{2}\min(\sigma,\bar{s})\in\mathbb{R}_{>0}

and
S:=|\bar{x}-c|
, where
\bar{x}
is any point such that
s<|\bar{x}-c|<\sigma
,
so that
0<\frac{s}{S}<1
and from (
4.2
) we obtain

\forall^{0}\varepsilon\,\forall j\in\mathbb{N}:\ \left|f_{\varepsilon}^{(j)}(c_{\varepsilon})\right|\leq K_{\varepsilon}\frac{j!}{S_{\varepsilon}^{j}}.

(4.3)

For each
[x_{\varepsilon}]\in B_{s}(c)
, we have

\displaystyle f_{\varepsilon}^{(n)}(x_{\varepsilon})

\displaystyle=\sum_{j=n}^{+\infty}\frac{f_{\varepsilon}^{(j)}(c_{\varepsilon})}{j!}n!{j\choose n}(x_{\varepsilon}-c_{\varepsilon})^{j-n},

and hence from (
4.3
):

\displaystyle\left|\frac{f_{\varepsilon}^{(n)}(x_{\varepsilon})}{n!}\right|

\displaystyle\leq\sum_{j=n}^{+\infty}K_{\varepsilon}{j\choose n}\frac{|x_{\varepsilon}-c_{\varepsilon}|^{j-n}}{S_{\varepsilon}^{j}}

\displaystyle\leq\frac{K_{\varepsilon}}{S_{\varepsilon}^{n}}\sum_{j=n}^{\infty}{j\choose n}\left(\frac{s_{\varepsilon}}{S_{\varepsilon}}\right)^{j-n}

\displaystyle=\frac{K_{\varepsilon}}{S_{\varepsilon}^{n}}\cdot\frac{1}{\left(1-\frac{s_{\varepsilon}}{S_{\varepsilon}}\right)^{n+1}}=\frac{K_{\varepsilon}}{\left(1-\frac{s_{\varepsilon}}{S_{\varepsilon}}\right)}\cdot\frac{1}{\left(S_{\varepsilon}\left(1-\frac{s_{\varepsilon}}{S_{\varepsilon}}\right)\right)^{n}},

which is our claim for
C:=\frac{K}{1-\frac{s}{S}}
and
R:=S\left(1-\frac{s}{S}\right)
.
Note that, differently with respect to the case of Colombeau real
analytic functions
[
26
]
, not necessarily the constant

\frac{1}{R}
is finite, e.g. if
s\approx S
.

We now prove that the condition is sufficient. Let
c=[c_{\varepsilon}]\in U

and
s=[s_{\varepsilon}]
,
C=[C_{\varepsilon}]
,
R=[R_{\varepsilon}]\in\mathchoice{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.6}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.56}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.47}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.35}{$\rho$}}}\widetilde{\mathbb{R}}_{>0}

be the constants satisfying (
4.1
). Set
\bar{s}:=\frac{1}{2}\min(s,R,\text{\rm rad}\left(\frac{f^{(n)}(c)}{n!}\right)_{\rm c})

and take
x\in B_{\bar{s}}(c)
. We first prove the equality
f(x)=\mathchoice{\mathchoice{{\vphantom{\sum}}{}^{\scalebox{0.6}{$\raisebox{-1.59442pt}{$\rho\hskip-5.49028pt$}$}}}{{\vphantom{\sum}}{}^{\scalebox{0.56}{$\raisebox{-1.59442pt}{$\rho\hskip-5.49028pt$}$}}}{{\vphantom{\sum}}{}^{\scalebox{0.47}{$\raisebox{-1.59442pt}{$\rho\hskip-5.49028pt$}$}}}{{\vphantom{\sum}}{}^{\scalebox{0.35}{$\raisebox{-1.59442pt}{$\rho\hskip-5.49028pt$}$}}}\sum_{n\in\mathchoice{{\vphantom{\mathbb{N}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.6}{$\sigma$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{N}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.56}{$\sigma$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{N}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.47}{$\sigma$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{N}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.35}{$\sigma$}}}\widetilde{\mathbb{N}}\,}}{\mathchoice{{\vphantom{\sum}}{}^{\scalebox{0.6}{$\rho\hskip-2.04861pt$}}}{{\vphantom{\sum}}{}^{\scalebox{0.56}{$\rho\hskip-2.04861pt$}}}{{\vphantom{\sum}}{}^{\scalebox{0.47}{$\rho\hskip-2.04861pt$}}}{{\vphantom{\sum}}{}^{\scalebox{0.35}{$\rho\hskip-2.04861pt$}}}\sum_{n\in\mathchoice{{\vphantom{\mathbb{N}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.6}{$\sigma$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{N}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.56}{$\sigma$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{N}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.47}{$\sigma$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{N}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.35}{$\sigma$}}}\widetilde{\mathbb{N}}\,}}{\mathchoice{{\vphantom{\sum}}{}^{\scalebox{0.6}{$\rho\hskip-2.04861pt$}}}{{\vphantom{\sum}}{}^{\scalebox{0.56}{$\rho\hskip-2.04861pt$}}}{{\vphantom{\sum}}{}^{\scalebox{0.47}{$\rho\hskip-2.04861pt$}}}{{\vphantom{\sum}}{}^{\scalebox{0.35}{$\rho\hskip-2.04861pt$}}}\sum_{n\in\mathchoice{{\vphantom{\mathbb{N}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.6}{$\sigma$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{N}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.56}{$\sigma$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{N}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.47}{$\sigma$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{N}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.35}{$\sigma$}}}\widetilde{\mathbb{N}}\,}}{\mathchoice{{\vphantom{\sum}}{}^{\scalebox{0.6}{$\rho\hskip-2.04861pt$}}}{{\vphantom{\sum}}{}^{\scalebox{0.56}{$\rho\hskip-2.04861pt$}}}{{\vphantom{\sum}}{}^{\scalebox{0.47}{$\rho\hskip-2.04861pt$}}}{{\vphantom{\sum}}{}^{\scalebox{0.35}{$\rho\hskip-2.04861pt$}}}\sum_{n\in\mathchoice{{\vphantom{\mathbb{N}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.6}{$\sigma$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{N}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.56}{$\sigma$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{N}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.47}{$\sigma$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{N}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.35}{$\sigma$}}}\widetilde{\mathbb{N}}\,}}\frac{f^{(n)}(c)}{n!}(x-c)^{n}
.
Let
N=[N_{\varepsilon}]\in\mathchoice{{\vphantom{\mathbb{N}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.6}{$\sigma$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{N}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.56}{$\sigma$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{N}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.47}{$\sigma$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{N}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.35}{$\sigma$}}}\widetilde{\mathbb{N}}
, with
N_{\varepsilon}\in\mathbb{N}
. For all
\varepsilon
,
from Taylor’s formula for the smooth
f_{\varepsilon}
, we have

\left|\sum_{n=0}^{N}\frac{f^{(n)}(c)}{n!}(x-c)^{n}-f(x)\right|=\left[\left|\frac{f_{\varepsilon}^{(N_{\varepsilon}+1)}(\xi_{\varepsilon})}{(N_{\varepsilon}+1)!}(x_{\varepsilon}-c_{\varepsilon})^{N_{\varepsilon}+1}\right|\right]

for some
t_{\varepsilon}\in[0,1]_{\mathbb{R}}
and for
\xi_{\varepsilon}:=(1-t_{\varepsilon})c_{\varepsilon}+t_{\varepsilon}x_{\varepsilon}
.
Since
\left|\xi_{\varepsilon}-c_{\varepsilon}\right|=t_{\varepsilon}\left|x_{\varepsilon}-c_{\varepsilon}\right|<\bar{s}_{\varepsilon}<s_{\varepsilon}
,
we can apply (
4.1
) and get
\forall^{0}\varepsilon\,\forall n\in\mathbb{N}:\ \left|\frac{f_{\varepsilon}^{(n)}(\xi_{\varepsilon})}{n!}\right|\leq\frac{C_{\varepsilon}}{R_{\varepsilon}^{n}}
.
Thereby, for these small
\varepsilon
and for
n=N_{\varepsilon}+1
we obtain

\left|\sum_{n=0}^{N}\frac{f^{(n)}(c)}{n!}(x-c)^{n}-f(x)\right|\leq C\left(\frac{\bar{s}}{R}\right)^{N+1},

and hence the claim follows by
\mathchoice{\mathchoice{{\vphantom{\lim}}{}^{\scalebox{0.6}{$\raisebox{-0.39864pt}{$\rho\hskip-5.49028pt$}$}}}{{\vphantom{\lim}}{}^{\scalebox{0.56}{$\raisebox{-0.39864pt}{$\rho\hskip-5.49028pt$}$}}}{{\vphantom{\lim}}{}^{\scalebox{0.47}{$\raisebox{-0.39864pt}{$\rho\hskip-5.49028pt$}$}}}{{\vphantom{\lim}}{}^{\scalebox{0.35}{$\raisebox{-0.39864pt}{$\rho\hskip-5.49028pt$}$}}}\lim_{n\in\mathchoice{{\vphantom{\mathbb{N}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.6}{$\sigma$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{N}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.56}{$\sigma$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{N}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.47}{$\sigma$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{N}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.35}{$\sigma$}}}\widetilde{\mathbb{N}}\,}}{\mathchoice{{\vphantom{\lim}}{}^{\scalebox{0.6}{$\rho\hskip-2.04861pt$}}}{{\vphantom{\lim}}{}^{\scalebox{0.56}{$\rho\hskip-2.04861pt$}}}{{\vphantom{\lim}}{}^{\scalebox{0.47}{$\rho\hskip-2.04861pt$}}}{{\vphantom{\lim}}{}^{\scalebox{0.35}{$\rho\hskip-2.04861pt$}}}\lim_{n\in\mathchoice{{\vphantom{\mathbb{N}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.6}{$\sigma$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{N}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.56}{$\sigma$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{N}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.47}{$\sigma$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{N}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.35}{$\sigma$}}}\widetilde{\mathbb{N}}\,}}{\mathchoice{{\vphantom{\lim}}{}^{\scalebox{0.6}{$\rho\hskip-2.04861pt$}}}{{\vphantom{\lim}}{}^{\scalebox{0.56}{$\rho\hskip-2.04861pt$}}}{{\vphantom{\lim}}{}^{\scalebox{0.47}{$\rho\hskip-2.04861pt$}}}{{\vphantom{\lim}}{}^{\scalebox{0.35}{$\rho\hskip-2.04861pt$}}}\lim_{n\in\mathchoice{{\vphantom{\mathbb{N}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.6}{$\sigma$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{N}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.56}{$\sigma$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{N}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.47}{$\sigma$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{N}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.35}{$\sigma$}}}\widetilde{\mathbb{N}}\,}}{\mathchoice{{\vphantom{\lim}}{}^{\scalebox{0.6}{$\rho\hskip-2.04861pt$}}}{{\vphantom{\lim}}{}^{\scalebox{0.56}{$\rho\hskip-2.04861pt$}}}{{\vphantom{\lim}}{}^{\scalebox{0.47}{$\rho\hskip-2.04861pt$}}}{{\vphantom{\lim}}{}^{\scalebox{0.35}{$\rho\hskip-2.04861pt$}}}\lim_{n\in\mathchoice{{\vphantom{\mathbb{N}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.6}{$\sigma$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{N}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.56}{$\sigma$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{N}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.47}{$\sigma$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{N}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.35}{$\sigma$}}}\widetilde{\mathbb{N}}\,}}\left(\frac{\bar{s}}{R}\right)^{N+1}=0
.

Now, we prove that
\mathchoice{\mathchoice{{\vphantom{\sum}}{}^{\scalebox{0.6}{$\raisebox{-1.59442pt}{$\rho\hskip-5.49028pt$}$}}}{{\vphantom{\sum}}{}^{\scalebox{0.56}{$\raisebox{-1.59442pt}{$\rho\hskip-5.49028pt$}$}}}{{\vphantom{\sum}}{}^{\scalebox{0.47}{$\raisebox{-1.59442pt}{$\rho\hskip-5.49028pt$}$}}}{{\vphantom{\sum}}{}^{\scalebox{0.35}{$\raisebox{-1.59442pt}{$\rho\hskip-5.49028pt$}$}}}\sum_{n\in\mathchoice{{\vphantom{\mathbb{N}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.6}{$\sigma$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{N}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.56}{$\sigma$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{N}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.47}{$\sigma$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{N}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.35}{$\sigma$}}}\widetilde{\mathbb{N}}\,}}{\mathchoice{{\vphantom{\sum}}{}^{\scalebox{0.6}{$\rho\hskip-2.04861pt$}}}{{\vphantom{\sum}}{}^{\scalebox{0.56}{$\rho\hskip-2.04861pt$}}}{{\vphantom{\sum}}{}^{\scalebox{0.47}{$\rho\hskip-2.04861pt$}}}{{\vphantom{\sum}}{}^{\scalebox{0.35}{$\rho\hskip-2.04861pt$}}}\sum_{n\in\mathchoice{{\vphantom{\mathbb{N}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.6}{$\sigma$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{N}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.56}{$\sigma$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{N}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.47}{$\sigma$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{N}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.35}{$\sigma$}}}\widetilde{\mathbb{N}}\,}}{\mathchoice{{\vphantom{\sum}}{}^{\scalebox{0.6}{$\rho\hskip-2.04861pt$}}}{{\vphantom{\sum}}{}^{\scalebox{0.56}{$\rho\hskip-2.04861pt$}}}{{\vphantom{\sum}}{}^{\scalebox{0.47}{$\rho\hskip-2.04861pt$}}}{{\vphantom{\sum}}{}^{\scalebox{0.35}{$\rho\hskip-2.04861pt$}}}\sum_{n\in\mathchoice{{\vphantom{\mathbb{N}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.6}{$\sigma$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{N}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.56}{$\sigma$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{N}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.47}{$\sigma$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{N}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.35}{$\sigma$}}}\widetilde{\mathbb{N}}\,}}{\mathchoice{{\vphantom{\sum}}{}^{\scalebox{0.6}{$\rho\hskip-2.04861pt$}}}{{\vphantom{\sum}}{}^{\scalebox{0.56}{$\rho\hskip-2.04861pt$}}}{{\vphantom{\sum}}{}^{\scalebox{0.47}{$\rho\hskip-2.04861pt$}}}{{\vphantom{\sum}}{}^{\scalebox{0.35}{$\rho\hskip-2.04861pt$}}}\sum_{n\in\mathchoice{{\vphantom{\mathbb{N}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.6}{$\sigma$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{N}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.56}{$\sigma$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{N}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.47}{$\sigma$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{N}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.35}{$\sigma$}}}\widetilde{\mathbb{N}}\,}}\frac{f^{(n)}(c)}{n!}(x-c)^{n}=\left[\sum_{n=0}^{+\infty}\frac{f_{\varepsilon}^{(n)}(c_{\varepsilon})}{n!}(x_{\varepsilon}-c_{\varepsilon})^{n}\right]
.
In fact, once again from (
4.1
) we have

\displaystyle\left|\sum_{n=0}^{N}\frac{f^{(n)}(c)}{n!}(x-c)^{n}-\left[\sum_{n=0}^{+\infty}\frac{f_{\varepsilon}^{(n)}(c_{\varepsilon})}{n!}(x_{\varepsilon}-c_{\varepsilon})^{n}\right]\right|

\displaystyle=\left[\left|\sum_{n=N_{\varepsilon}+1}^{+\infty}\frac{f_{\varepsilon}^{(n)}(c_{\varepsilon})}{n!}(x_{\varepsilon}-c_{\varepsilon})^{n}\right|\right]

\displaystyle\leq\left[\sum_{n=N_{\varepsilon}+1}^{+\infty}\frac{C_{\varepsilon}}{R_{\varepsilon}^{n}}\left|x_{\varepsilon}-c_{\varepsilon}\right|^{n}\right]

\displaystyle\leq C\cdot\ \ \mathchoice{\mathchoice{{\vphantom{\sum}}{}^{\scalebox{0.6}{$\raisebox{-1.59442pt}{$\rho\hskip-18.0278pt$}$}}}{{\vphantom{\sum}}{}^{\scalebox{0.56}{$\raisebox{-1.59442pt}{$\rho\hskip-18.0278pt$}$}}}{{\vphantom{\sum}}{}^{\scalebox{0.47}{$\raisebox{-1.59442pt}{$\rho\hskip-18.0278pt$}$}}}{{\vphantom{\sum}}{}^{\scalebox{0.35}{$\raisebox{-1.59442pt}{$\rho\hskip-18.0278pt$}$}}}\sum_{n\in\mathchoice{{\vphantom{\mathbb{N}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.6}{$\sigma$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{N}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.56}{$\sigma$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{N}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.47}{$\sigma$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{N}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.35}{$\sigma$}}}\widetilde{\mathbb{N}}_{\geq N+1}\,}}{\mathchoice{{\vphantom{\sum}}{}^{\scalebox{0.6}{$\rho\hskip-2.04861pt$}}}{{\vphantom{\sum}}{}^{\scalebox{0.56}{$\rho\hskip-2.04861pt$}}}{{\vphantom{\sum}}{}^{\scalebox{0.47}{$\rho\hskip-2.04861pt$}}}{{\vphantom{\sum}}{}^{\scalebox{0.35}{$\rho\hskip-2.04861pt$}}}\sum_{n\in\mathchoice{{\vphantom{\mathbb{N}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.6}{$\sigma$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{N}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.56}{$\sigma$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{N}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.47}{$\sigma$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{N}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.35}{$\sigma$}}}\widetilde{\mathbb{N}}_{\geq N+1}\,}}{\mathchoice{{\vphantom{\sum}}{}^{\scalebox{0.6}{$\rho\hskip-2.04861pt$}}}{{\vphantom{\sum}}{}^{\scalebox{0.56}{$\rho\hskip-2.04861pt$}}}{{\vphantom{\sum}}{}^{\scalebox{0.47}{$\rho\hskip-2.04861pt$}}}{{\vphantom{\sum}}{}^{\scalebox{0.35}{$\rho\hskip-2.04861pt$}}}\sum_{n\in\mathchoice{{\vphantom{\mathbb{N}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.6}{$\sigma$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{N}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.56}{$\sigma$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{N}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.47}{$\sigma$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{N}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.35}{$\sigma$}}}\widetilde{\mathbb{N}}_{\geq N+1}\,}}{\mathchoice{{\vphantom{\sum}}{}^{\scalebox{0.6}{$\rho\hskip-2.04861pt$}}}{{\vphantom{\sum}}{}^{\scalebox{0.56}{$\rho\hskip-2.04861pt$}}}{{\vphantom{\sum}}{}^{\scalebox{0.47}{$\rho\hskip-2.04861pt$}}}{{\vphantom{\sum}}{}^{\scalebox{0.35}{$\rho\hskip-2.04861pt$}}}\sum_{n\in\mathchoice{{\vphantom{\mathbb{N}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.6}{$\sigma$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{N}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.56}{$\sigma$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{N}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.47}{$\sigma$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{N}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.35}{$\sigma$}}}\widetilde{\mathbb{N}}_{\geq N+1}\,}}\left(\frac{\bar{s}}{R}\right)^{n}\to 0

because
\bar{s}<R
and hence
\mathchoice{\mathchoice{{\vphantom{\sum}}{}^{\scalebox{0.6}{$\raisebox{-1.59442pt}{$\rho\hskip-5.49028pt$}$}}}{{\vphantom{\sum}}{}^{\scalebox{0.56}{$\raisebox{-1.59442pt}{$\rho\hskip-5.49028pt$}$}}}{{\vphantom{\sum}}{}^{\scalebox{0.47}{$\raisebox{-1.59442pt}{$\rho\hskip-5.49028pt$}$}}}{{\vphantom{\sum}}{}^{\scalebox{0.35}{$\raisebox{-1.59442pt}{$\rho\hskip-5.49028pt$}$}}}\sum_{n\in\mathchoice{{\vphantom{\mathbb{N}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.6}{$\sigma$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{N}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.56}{$\sigma$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{N}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.47}{$\sigma$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{N}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.35}{$\sigma$}}}\widetilde{\mathbb{N}}\,}}{\mathchoice{{\vphantom{\sum}}{}^{\scalebox{0.6}{$\rho\hskip-2.04861pt$}}}{{\vphantom{\sum}}{}^{\scalebox{0.56}{$\rho\hskip-2.04861pt$}}}{{\vphantom{\sum}}{}^{\scalebox{0.47}{$\rho\hskip-2.04861pt$}}}{{\vphantom{\sum}}{}^{\scalebox{0.35}{$\rho\hskip-2.04861pt$}}}\sum_{n\in\mathchoice{{\vphantom{\mathbb{N}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.6}{$\sigma$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{N}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.56}{$\sigma$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{N}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.47}{$\sigma$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{N}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.35}{$\sigma$}}}\widetilde{\mathbb{N}}\,}}{\mathchoice{{\vphantom{\sum}}{}^{\scalebox{0.6}{$\rho\hskip-2.04861pt$}}}{{\vphantom{\sum}}{}^{\scalebox{0.56}{$\rho\hskip-2.04861pt$}}}{{\vphantom{\sum}}{}^{\scalebox{0.47}{$\rho\hskip-2.04861pt$}}}{{\vphantom{\sum}}{}^{\scalebox{0.35}{$\rho\hskip-2.04861pt$}}}\sum_{n\in\mathchoice{{\vphantom{\mathbb{N}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.6}{$\sigma$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{N}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.56}{$\sigma$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{N}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.47}{$\sigma$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{N}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.35}{$\sigma$}}}\widetilde{\mathbb{N}}\,}}{\mathchoice{{\vphantom{\sum}}{}^{\scalebox{0.6}{$\rho\hskip-2.04861pt$}}}{{\vphantom{\sum}}{}^{\scalebox{0.56}{$\rho\hskip-2.04861pt$}}}{{\vphantom{\sum}}{}^{\scalebox{0.47}{$\rho\hskip-2.04861pt$}}}{{\vphantom{\sum}}{}^{\scalebox{0.35}{$\rho\hskip-2.04861pt$}}}\sum_{n\in\mathchoice{{\vphantom{\mathbb{N}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.6}{$\sigma$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{N}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.56}{$\sigma$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{N}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.47}{$\sigma$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{N}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.35}{$\sigma$}}}\widetilde{\mathbb{N}}\,}}\left(\frac{\bar{s}}{R}\right)^{n}

converges. Finally, take
\bar{x}\in B_{\bar{s}}(c)
such that
|x-c|<|\bar{x}-c|
.
As above, we can prove that
\mathchoice{\mathchoice{{\vphantom{\sum}}{}^{\scalebox{0.6}{$\raisebox{-1.59442pt}{$\rho\hskip-5.49028pt$}$}}}{{\vphantom{\sum}}{}^{\scalebox{0.56}{$\raisebox{-1.59442pt}{$\rho\hskip-5.49028pt$}$}}}{{\vphantom{\sum}}{}^{\scalebox{0.47}{$\raisebox{-1.59442pt}{$\rho\hskip-5.49028pt$}$}}}{{\vphantom{\sum}}{}^{\scalebox{0.35}{$\raisebox{-1.59442pt}{$\rho\hskip-5.49028pt$}$}}}\sum_{n\in\mathchoice{{\vphantom{\mathbb{N}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.6}{$\sigma$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{N}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.56}{$\sigma$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{N}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.47}{$\sigma$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{N}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.35}{$\sigma$}}}\widetilde{\mathbb{N}}\,}}{\mathchoice{{\vphantom{\sum}}{}^{\scalebox{0.6}{$\rho\hskip-2.04861pt$}}}{{\vphantom{\sum}}{}^{\scalebox{0.56}{$\rho\hskip-2.04861pt$}}}{{\vphantom{\sum}}{}^{\scalebox{0.47}{$\rho\hskip-2.04861pt$}}}{{\vphantom{\sum}}{}^{\scalebox{0.35}{$\rho\hskip-2.04861pt$}}}\sum_{n\in\mathchoice{{\vphantom{\mathbb{N}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.6}{$\sigma$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{N}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.56}{$\sigma$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{N}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.47}{$\sigma$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{N}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.35}{$\sigma$}}}\widetilde{\mathbb{N}}\,}}{\mathchoice{{\vphantom{\sum}}{}^{\scalebox{0.6}{$\rho\hskip-2.04861pt$}}}{{\vphantom{\sum}}{}^{\scalebox{0.56}{$\rho\hskip-2.04861pt$}}}{{\vphantom{\sum}}{}^{\scalebox{0.47}{$\rho\hskip-2.04861pt$}}}{{\vphantom{\sum}}{}^{\scalebox{0.35}{$\rho\hskip-2.04861pt$}}}\sum_{n\in\mathchoice{{\vphantom{\mathbb{N}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.6}{$\sigma$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{N}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.56}{$\sigma$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{N}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.47}{$\sigma$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{N}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.35}{$\sigma$}}}\widetilde{\mathbb{N}}\,}}{\mathchoice{{\vphantom{\sum}}{}^{\scalebox{0.6}{$\rho\hskip-2.04861pt$}}}{{\vphantom{\sum}}{}^{\scalebox{0.56}{$\rho\hskip-2.04861pt$}}}{{\vphantom{\sum}}{}^{\scalebox{0.47}{$\rho\hskip-2.04861pt$}}}{{\vphantom{\sum}}{}^{\scalebox{0.35}{$\rho\hskip-2.04861pt$}}}\sum_{n\in\mathchoice{{\vphantom{\mathbb{N}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.6}{$\sigma$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{N}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.56}{$\sigma$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{N}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.47}{$\sigma$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{N}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.35}{$\sigma$}}}\widetilde{\mathbb{N}}\,}}\frac{f^{(n)}(c)}{n!}(\bar{x}-c)^{n}

converges; moreover from (
4.1
) we also have
\forall^{0}\varepsilon\,\forall n\in\mathbb{N}:\ \left|\frac{f_{\varepsilon}^{(n)}(c_{\varepsilon})}{n!}(\bar{x}_{\varepsilon}-c_{\varepsilon})^{n}\right|\leq C_{\varepsilon}\left(\frac{\bar{s}_{\varepsilon}}{R_{\varepsilon}}\right)^{n}\leq\frac{C_{\varepsilon}}{1-\frac{\bar{s}_{\varepsilon}}{R_{\varepsilon}}}
.
This proves that
\left(\frac{f^{(n)}(c)}{n!}(\bar{x}-c)^{n}\right)_{n\in\mathbb{N}}

is eventually
\mathchoice{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.6}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.56}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.47}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.35}{$\rho$}}}\widetilde{\mathbb{R}}
-bounded in
\mathchoice{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.6}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.56}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.47}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.35}{$\rho$}}}\widetilde{\mathbb{R}}_{\text{\rm c}}
and hence
x\in\mathchoice{{\vphantom{\text{\rm c}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.6}{$\rho$}}_{\scalebox{0.6}{$\sigma$}}}{{\vphantom{\text{\rm c}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.56}{$\rho$}}_{\scalebox{0.56}{$\sigma$}}}{{\vphantom{\text{\rm c}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.47}{$\rho$}}_{\scalebox{0.47}{$\sigma$}}}{{\vphantom{\text{\rm c}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.35}{$\rho$}}_{\scalebox{0.35}{$\sigma$}}}\text{\rm conv}\left(\left(\frac{f^{(n)}(c)}{n!}\right)_{\text{\rm c}},c\right)

by Cor.
25
.
∎

As we have already noted in this proof, differently with respect to
the definition of Colombeau real analytic function
[
26
]
,
we have that, generally speaking,
\frac{1}{R}\in\mathchoice{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.6}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.56}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.47}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.35}{$\rho$}}}\widetilde{\mathbb{R}}
is not finite.
For example, for
f=\delta
at
c=0
, we have
\left|\frac{\delta_{\varepsilon}^{(n)}(x_{\varepsilon})}{n!}\right|=\left|\frac{\mu^{(n)}(x_{\varepsilon})}{n!}b_{\varepsilon}^{n+1}\right|=\left|\frac{\mu^{(n)}(x_{\varepsilon})}{n!}b_{\varepsilon}\right|\frac{1}{\left(b_{\varepsilon}^{-1}\right)^{n}}\leq\frac{\bar{C}b_{\varepsilon}}{(b_{\varepsilon}^{-1})^{n}}
,
where
\left|\mu^{(n)}(x_{\varepsilon})\right|\leq\int\beta=:\bar{C}
and
hence
\frac{1}{R}=b
which is an infinite number. Thereby, in the
particular case when
\frac{1}{R}
is finite,
f
is a Colombeau
real analytic function in a neighborhood of
c
. Vice versa, any
Colombeau real analytic function and any ordinary real analytic function
are GRAF.

This characterization also yields the closure of GRAF with respect
to inversion. We first recall that the local inverse function theorem
holds for GSF, see
[
10
]
. Therefore, if
f\in\mathchoice{{\vphantom{\mathcal{G}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.6}{$\rho$}}_{\scalebox{0.6}{$\sigma$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathcal{G}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.56}{$\rho$}}_{\scalebox{0.56}{$\sigma$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathcal{G}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.47}{$\rho$}}_{\scalebox{0.47}{$\sigma$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathcal{G}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.35}{$\rho$}}_{\scalebox{0.35}{$\sigma$}}}\mathcal{GC}^{\omega}(U,\mathchoice{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.6}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.56}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.47}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.35}{$\rho$}}}\widetilde{\mathbb{R}})\subseteq\mathchoice{{\vphantom{\mathcal{G}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.6}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathcal{G}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.56}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathcal{G}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.47}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathcal{G}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.35}{$\rho$}}}\mathcal{GC}^{\infty}(U,\mathchoice{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.6}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.56}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.47}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.35}{$\rho$}}}\widetilde{\mathbb{R}})

and at the point
x_{0}\in U
the derivative
f^{\prime}(x_{0})
is invertible,
we can find open neighborhoods of
x_{0}\in X\subseteq U
and of

y_{0}:=f(x_{0})\in Y
such that
f|_{X}:X\to Y
is invertible,

\left(f|_{X}\right)^{-1}\in\mathchoice{{\vphantom{\mathcal{G}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.6}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathcal{G}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.56}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathcal{G}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.47}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathcal{G}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.35}{$\rho$}}}\mathcal{GC}^{\infty}(Y,X)
and
f^{\prime}(x)
is invertible
for all
x\in X
.

Theorem 38
.

If
\sigma\leq\rho^{*}
and we use notations and assumptions introduced
above, then
\left(f|_{X}\right)^{-1}\in\mathchoice{{\vphantom{\mathcal{G}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.6}{$\rho$}}_{\scalebox{0.6}{$\sigma$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathcal{G}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.56}{$\rho$}}_{\scalebox{0.56}{$\sigma$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathcal{G}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.47}{$\rho$}}_{\scalebox{0.47}{$\sigma$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathcal{G}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.35}{$\rho$}}_{\scalebox{0.35}{$\sigma$}}}\mathcal{GC}^{\omega}(Y,X)
.

Proof.

For simplicity, set
g:=\left(f|_{X}\right)^{-1}
and
h(x):=\frac{1}{f^{\prime}(x)}

for all
x\in X
, so that
g^{\prime}(y)=h[g(y)]
for all
y\in Y
. From
Cor.
32
and Thm.
26
, we know
that
h
is a GRAF. Therefore, Thm.
37
yields

\forall^{0}\varepsilon\,\forall n\in\mathbb{N}:\ \left|h_{\varepsilon}^{(j)}(x_{\varepsilon})\right|\leq C_{\varepsilon}\frac{j!}{R_{\varepsilon}^{j}}

for all
[x_{\varepsilon}]\in B_{s}(x_{0})
and suitable constants
s
,

C
,
R\in\mathchoice{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.6}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.56}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.47}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.35}{$\rho$}}}\widetilde{\mathbb{R}}_{>0}
. For
[y_{\varepsilon}]\in f\left(B_{s}(x_{0})\right)

(note that this is an open neighborhood of
y_{0}
because
f
is
an open map) and these
\varepsilon
, formula (1.15) of
[
22
, Thm.1.5.3]

yields
\left|g_{\varepsilon}^{(j)}(y_{\varepsilon})\right|\leq j!(-1)^{j-1}{1/2\choose j}\frac{(2C_{\varepsilon})^{j}}{R_{\varepsilon}^{j-1}}

for all
j\in\mathbb{N}_{>0}
, and hence
g\in\mathchoice{{\vphantom{\mathcal{G}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.6}{$\rho$}}_{\scalebox{0.6}{$\sigma$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathcal{G}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.56}{$\rho$}}_{\scalebox{0.56}{$\sigma$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathcal{G}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.47}{$\rho$}}_{\scalebox{0.47}{$\sigma$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathcal{G}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.35}{$\rho$}}_{\scalebox{0.35}{$\sigma$}}}\mathcal{GC}^{\omega}(U,\mathchoice{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.6}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.56}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.47}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.35}{$\rho$}}}\widetilde{\mathbb{R}})
once again
by Thm.
37
.
∎

Since
\delta
is a GRAF, in general the identity principle does
not hold for GRAF. From our point of view this is a feature of GRAF
because it allows to include as GRAF a large class of interesting
generalized functions and hence pave the way to a more general related
Cauchy-Kowalevski theorem. The following theorem clearly shows that
the identity principle does not hold in our framework exactly because
we are in a non-Archimedean setting: every interval is not connected
in the sharp topology because the set of all the infinitesimals is
a clopen set, see e.g.
[
9
]
.

Theorem 39
.

Let
U\subseteq\mathchoice{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.6}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.56}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.47}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.35}{$\rho$}}}\widetilde{\mathbb{R}}
be an open set and

f
,
g\in\mathchoice{{\vphantom{\mathcal{G}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.6}{$\rho$}}_{\scalebox{0.6}{$\sigma$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathcal{G}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.56}{$\rho$}}_{\scalebox{0.56}{$\sigma$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathcal{G}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.47}{$\rho$}}_{\scalebox{0.47}{$\sigma$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathcal{G}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.35}{$\rho$}}_{\scalebox{0.35}{$\sigma$}}}\mathcal{GC}^{\omega}(U,\mathchoice{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.6}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.56}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.47}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.35}{$\rho$}}}\widetilde{\mathbb{R}})
. Then the set

\mathcal{O}:=\text{\emph{int}}\left\{x\in U\mid f(x)=g(x)\right\}

is clopen in the sharp topology.

Proof.

For simplicity, considering
f-g
, without loss of generality we
can assume
g=0
. We only have to show that
\mathcal{O}
is closed
in
U
. Assume that
c
is in the closure of
\mathcal{O}
in
U
,
i.e.

c\in U,\ \forall r\in\mathchoice{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.6}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.56}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.47}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.35}{$\rho$}}}\widetilde{\mathbb{R}}_{>0}\,\exists\bar{c}\in B_{r}(c)\cap\mathcal{O}.

(4.4)

We have to prove that
c\in\mathcal{O}
. We first note that for each

\bar{c}\in\mathcal{O}
, we have
B_{p}(\bar{c})\subseteq\mathcal{O}

for some
p\in\mathchoice{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.6}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.56}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.47}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.35}{$\rho$}}}\widetilde{\mathbb{R}}_{>0}
and hence

f(\bar{x})=0\quad\forall\bar{x}\in B_{p}(\bar{c}).

(4.5)

Now, fix
n\in\mathbb{N}
in order to prove that
f^{(n)}(c)=0
. From (
4.4
),
for all
r\in\mathchoice{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.6}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.56}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.47}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.35}{$\rho$}}}\widetilde{\mathbb{R}}_{>0}
we can find
\bar{c}_{r}\in B_{r}(c)\cap\mathcal{O}

such that
f^{(n)}(\bar{c}_{r})=0
from (
4.5
). From
sharp continuity of
f^{(n)}
, we have
f^{(n)}(c)=\lim_{r\to 0^{+}}f^{(n)}(\bar{c}_{r})=0
.
Since
f\in\mathchoice{{\vphantom{\mathcal{G}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.6}{$\rho$}}_{\scalebox{0.6}{$\sigma$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathcal{G}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.56}{$\rho$}}_{\scalebox{0.56}{$\sigma$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathcal{G}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.47}{$\rho$}}_{\scalebox{0.47}{$\sigma$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathcal{G}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.35}{$\rho$}}_{\scalebox{0.35}{$\sigma$}}}\mathcal{GC}^{\omega}(U,\mathchoice{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.6}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.56}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.47}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.35}{$\rho$}}}\widetilde{\mathbb{R}})
and
c\in U
, we can hence find
\sigma>0

such that
f(x)=\mathchoice{\mathchoice{{\vphantom{\sum}}{}^{\scalebox{0.6}{$\raisebox{-1.59442pt}{$\rho\hskip-5.49028pt$}$}}}{{\vphantom{\sum}}{}^{\scalebox{0.56}{$\raisebox{-1.59442pt}{$\rho\hskip-5.49028pt$}$}}}{{\vphantom{\sum}}{}^{\scalebox{0.47}{$\raisebox{-1.59442pt}{$\rho\hskip-5.49028pt$}$}}}{{\vphantom{\sum}}{}^{\scalebox{0.35}{$\raisebox{-1.59442pt}{$\rho\hskip-5.49028pt$}$}}}\sum_{n\in\mathchoice{{\vphantom{\mathbb{N}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.6}{$\sigma$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{N}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.56}{$\sigma$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{N}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.47}{$\sigma$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{N}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.35}{$\sigma$}}}\widetilde{\mathbb{N}}\,}}{\mathchoice{{\vphantom{\sum}}{}^{\scalebox{0.6}{$\rho\hskip-2.04861pt$}}}{{\vphantom{\sum}}{}^{\scalebox{0.56}{$\rho\hskip-2.04861pt$}}}{{\vphantom{\sum}}{}^{\scalebox{0.47}{$\rho\hskip-2.04861pt$}}}{{\vphantom{\sum}}{}^{\scalebox{0.35}{$\rho\hskip-2.04861pt$}}}\sum_{n\in\mathchoice{{\vphantom{\mathbb{N}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.6}{$\sigma$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{N}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.56}{$\sigma$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{N}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.47}{$\sigma$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{N}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.35}{$\sigma$}}}\widetilde{\mathbb{N}}\,}}{\mathchoice{{\vphantom{\sum}}{}^{\scalebox{0.6}{$\rho\hskip-2.04861pt$}}}{{\vphantom{\sum}}{}^{\scalebox{0.56}{$\rho\hskip-2.04861pt$}}}{{\vphantom{\sum}}{}^{\scalebox{0.47}{$\rho\hskip-2.04861pt$}}}{{\vphantom{\sum}}{}^{\scalebox{0.35}{$\rho\hskip-2.04861pt$}}}\sum_{n\in\mathchoice{{\vphantom{\mathbb{N}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.6}{$\sigma$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{N}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.56}{$\sigma$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{N}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.47}{$\sigma$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{N}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.35}{$\sigma$}}}\widetilde{\mathbb{N}}\,}}{\mathchoice{{\vphantom{\sum}}{}^{\scalebox{0.6}{$\rho\hskip-2.04861pt$}}}{{\vphantom{\sum}}{}^{\scalebox{0.56}{$\rho\hskip-2.04861pt$}}}{{\vphantom{\sum}}{}^{\scalebox{0.47}{$\rho\hskip-2.04861pt$}}}{{\vphantom{\sum}}{}^{\scalebox{0.35}{$\rho\hskip-2.04861pt$}}}\sum_{n\in\mathchoice{{\vphantom{\mathbb{N}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.6}{$\sigma$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{N}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.56}{$\sigma$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{N}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.47}{$\sigma$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{N}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.35}{$\sigma$}}}\widetilde{\mathbb{N}}\,}}\frac{f^{(n)}(c)}{n!}\left(x-c\right){}^{n}=0

for all
x\in B_{\sigma}(c)
, i.e.
c\in\mathcal{O}
.
∎

For example, if
f=\delta
and
g=0
, the set

\text{int}\left\{x\in\mathchoice{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.6}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.56}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.47}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.35}{$\rho$}}}\widetilde{\mathbb{R}}\mid\delta(x)=0\right\}\supseteq\left\{x\in\mathchoice{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.6}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.56}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.47}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.35}{$\rho$}}}\widetilde{\mathbb{R}}\mid|x|\gg 0\right\}

is clopen. Thereby, also
\mathchoice{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.6}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.56}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.47}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.35}{$\rho$}}}\widetilde{\mathbb{R}}\setminus\text{int}\left\{x\in\mathchoice{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.6}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.56}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.47}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.35}{$\rho$}}}\widetilde{\mathbb{R}}\mid\delta(x)=0\right\}

is clopen, and we have

\displaystyle\left\{x\in\mathchoice{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.6}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.56}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.47}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.35}{$\rho$}}}\widetilde{\mathbb{R}}\mid\delta(x)\neq 0\right\}

\displaystyle\subseteq\mathchoice{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.6}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.56}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.47}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.35}{$\rho$}}}\widetilde{\mathbb{R}}\setminus\text{int}\left\{x\in\mathchoice{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.6}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.56}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.47}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.35}{$\rho$}}}\widetilde{\mathbb{R}}\mid\delta(x)=0\right\}

\displaystyle\subseteq\left\{x\in\mathchoice{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.6}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.56}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.47}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.35}{$\rho$}}}\widetilde{\mathbb{R}}\mid\forall r\in\mathbb{R}_{>0}:\ |x|\leq_{\text{s}}r\right\}.

If we assume that all the derivatives of
f
are finite and the neighborhoods
of Def.
30
are also finite, then we can repeat the previous
proof considering only standard points
c\in\mathbb{R}
and radii
r\in\mathbb{R}_{>0}
,
obtaining the following sufficient condition:

Theorem 40
.

Let
U\subseteq\mathchoice{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.6}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.56}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.47}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.35}{$\rho$}}}\widetilde{\mathbb{R}}
be an open set
such that
U\cap\mathbb{R}
is connected. Let
f
,
g\in\mathchoice{{\vphantom{\mathcal{G}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.6}{$\rho$}}_{\scalebox{0.6}{$\sigma$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathcal{G}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.56}{$\rho$}}_{\scalebox{0.56}{$\sigma$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathcal{G}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.47}{$\rho$}}_{\scalebox{0.47}{$\sigma$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathcal{G}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.35}{$\rho$}}_{\scalebox{0.35}{$\sigma$}}}\mathcal{GC}^{\omega}(U,\mathchoice{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.6}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.56}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.47}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.35}{$\rho$}}}\widetilde{\mathbb{R}})

be such that
f|_{V\cap\mathbb{R}}=g|_{V\cap\mathbb{R}}
for some nonempty subset

V\subseteq U
such that
V\cap\mathbb{R}
is open in the Fermat topology,
i.e.

\forall x\in V\cap\mathbb{R}\,\exists r\in\mathbb{R}_{>0}:\ B_{r}(x)\subseteq V\cap\mathbb{R}.

Finally, assume that all the following quantities are finite:

(i)

The neighborhood length
s
in Def.
30

is finite for each
c\in U\cap\mathbb{R}
,

(ii)

\forall x\in U\,\forall n\in\mathbb{N}:\ f^{(n)}(x)

and
g^{(n)}(x)
are finite.

Then
f|_{U\cap\mathbb{R}}=g|_{U\cap\mathbb{R}}
.

Proof.

The proof proceeds exactly as in Thm.
39
but
considering

\mathcal{O}:=\text{int}_{\text{F}}\left\{x\in U\cap\mathbb{R}\mid f(x)=g(x)\right\},

where
\text{int}_{\text{F}}
is the interior in the Fermat topology
(i.e. the topology generated by the balls
B_{r}(c)
for
c\in\mathchoice{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.6}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.56}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.47}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.35}{$\rho$}}}\widetilde{\mathbb{R}}

and
r\in\mathbb{R}_{>0}
, see
[
9
]
). We have to note that assumption

(ii)
implies that all
f^{(n)}
are continuous in
this topology (see
[
12
]
).
∎

For example, if
f\in\mathcal{C}^{\omega}(\mathbb{R})
is an ordinary
real analytic function and
K
,
h\in\mathchoice{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.6}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.56}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.47}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.35}{$\rho$}}}\widetilde{\mathbb{R}}
are finite numbers, the
GRAF
x\in\text{int}({\text{\rm c}}({\mathchoice{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.6}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.56}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.47}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.35}{$\rho$}}}\widetilde{\mathbb{R}}}))\mapsto Kf(hx)\in\mathchoice{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.6}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.56}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.47}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.35}{$\rho$}}}\widetilde{\mathbb{R}}
, where
{\text{\rm c}}({\mathchoice{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.6}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.56}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.47}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.35}{$\rho$}}}\widetilde{\mathbb{R}}})

is the set of compactly supported points, satisfies the assumptions
of the last theorem.

5.
Conclusions

Sometimes, e.g. in the study of PDE, the class of real analytic functions
is described as a too rigid set of solutions. In spite of their good
properties with respect to algebraic operations, composition, differentiation,
integration, inversion, etc., this rigidity is essentially well represented
by the identity principle that necessarily excludes e.g. solitons
with compact support or interesting generalized functions. Thanks
to Thm.
39
, we can state that this rigidity
is due to the banishing of non-Archimedean numbers from mathematical
analysis. The use of hyperseries allows one to recover all these features
including also interesting non trivial generalized functions and compactly
supported functions. This paves the way for an interesting generalization
of the Cauchy-Kowalevski theorem for GRAF that we intend to develop
in a subsequent work. Its proof can be approached by trying a generalization
of the classical method of majorants, or using the Picard-Lindelöf
theorem for PDE with GSF and then using characterization Thm.
37

to show that the GSF solution is actually a GRAF.
